## 2026-11-13 — Session 263: two aligned lines still promised different numbers

**Integration / sequencing.** Main had already allocated Schema v154 and ADR-0194 to Chargeback Period Posting. To merge both independently-built, additive capabilities without overwriting either migration, the cross-line Goal Commitment magnitude follow-on is now [ADR-0195](./docs/adr/0195-cross-line-goal-commitment-target-magnitudes.md), Schema v154 → v155. The v153 → v154 chargeback migration remains intact; the v154 → v155 migration adds only blank target fields and target conventions to retained goal commitments, inferring nothing.

**Built.** A commitment may now declare canonical exact non-negative decimal `targetValue` plus `targetUnit`, together or absent together and only with measure/direction. For two overlapping named lines with an aligned measure/direction and identical declared unit, `buildGoalCommitments` scales both values using `BigInt` and cross-multiplies the governed divergence floor (`goalCommitmentMagnitudeDivergencePct`, default 10%). A threshold-reaching divergence is `magnitude-conflict` and routes through `RULE-GCC-01` to the unique lowest joint apex. Missing values (`magnitude-undeclared`) and unit mismatch (`magnitude-unit-mismatch`) remain named refusals; no value, unit, conversion, or prevailing target is inferred. Raw target data is authorised-pair-only; the aggregate brief and CSV #145 expose counts, never the values or units.

**No-line integrity correction.** An explicit `no-line` declaration remains live in the person's exact capacity sweep and retains `single-principal` pair semantics. It is never given a guessed holder or a target-magnitude finding. If it contributes to an overcommitted peak, the owner row, roll-up and rule now withhold a partial named-holder apex: capacity arithmetic remains visible, while `RULE-GCC-01` records one identity-free `unattributed` observation and files no partial delegation.

**Verification.** Integration run: `core` + `csv` + `goal-commitment` + `chargeback-posting` **62/62 PASS**. The focused goal register is **36/36 PASS** and the Chargeback Period Posting suite **6/6 PASS** under Schema v155. `npm run lint` PASS; canonical boundary PASS (827 app ES modules / 245 test suites / 23 documentation checks); docs links PASS (1,152 links / 312 Markdown files); standalone build and standalone suite **7/7 PASS**. Full repository suite and real-browser/AT walkthrough remain **[UNVERIFIED]**.

## 2026-11-13 — Session 259: Cross-Line Priority Ballot & Social-Choice Aggregation (ADR-0191, Schema v151, RULE-SOC-01, CSV #144, `#/matrix?panel=priority`)

**Doctrine / scope.** Director / VP / CEO PRIMARY × **matrix** — the rotation Session 258 named. Single-line workspaces are served by the same reader and read `inert` with the reason printed. One item, shipped end to end: a round that freezes a person, their live items and their reporting lines; one strict complete ballot per frozen line; and a social-choice aggregate that is exact, refuses to overclaim, and routes a genuine disagreement to the lines rather than the person.

**Gap discovery (Step 3).** The build closed the Planned gap logged at Session 244 (`condorcet|schulze|kemeny|ranked ballot|social choice` matched 0 files under `app/`). NEW gap logged to Planned: a **cross-line goal commitment conflict register** — two managers of one person can each set a goal with its own target and date and nothing compares the commitments; `goalCarriage` (Session 225) only counts a goal once and ADR-0143 only compares dated work items. Evidence: `goal conflict|commitment conflict` 0 hits in `app/` and 0 lines in ROADMAP; `app/core/domain/goals.js` has no `reportingLines` / `buildMatrix` / `matrixJointApex` reference.

**Benchmark (Step 3.5) — [BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED].** Comparators: deliberative-polling / ranked-choice products (RankedVote, OpinionX, Pol.is) and Condorcet counting libraries (OpenSTV/EL). Vendor sites were not reachable from the sandbox, so the comparison is from general knowledge of the category, not a feature check made today. Believed matched: strict complete rankings, Condorcet / Schulze / Kemeny tallying, cycle and tie disclosure. Believed exceeded: voters are the frozen holders of **named lines** (not a self-selected audience), a later reorganisation cannot rewrite who was asked or on what set, coverage and its Wilson interval are published as a claim separate from the result, no result is claimed below the ballot floor, and a cycle is addressed to the lines' joint apex rather than shown on a results page. Known weaker: no live/simultaneous voting, no invitations or reminders, no tied-rank/partial ballots, no weighted or delegated voting, no elimination rounds, no public links — all logged In Progress.

**Built (one item, end to end).** `priorityRankingRegistry.js` (closed verdict / coverage / refusal vocabularies with `says`, bounds, six named frameworks with `notSaid`, method text, CSV descriptor, rights, events) · `priorityRankingSchema.js` (fail-closed round/ballot normalizers, per-round uniqueness, clamp-on-load conventions) · `priorityRanking.js` (the ONE reader: pairwise counts, Condorcet, Tarjan strict components → Schwartz top cycle, Floyd–Warshall Schulze strengths, exact Kemeny–Young with tie/cap verdicts, per-line coverage, per-holder and joint-apex roll-ups, Wilson coverage with a floor) · `priorityRankingRules.js` (`RULE-SOC-01`) · `priorityRankingActions.js` (declare / cast / withdraw / conventions / scan with rank and line checks) · `app/views/matrixPriorityPanel.js` (Board · Ballots · Method, mounted from `matrix.js`) · schema v151 with `MIGRATIONS[150]` creating both ledgers empty · CSV scope #144 · `postCommit`, `propagationContracts`, `matrixRegistry`, `seatRegistry`, `pausePermitRegistry`, `accessGateway`, `actions.js`, `csv.js` wiring.

**Defects found & fixed during the build.** (1) The four new conventions and their pause switch were **dropped on every reload**: `normalizeSettings` rebuilds settings field by field, and nothing spread `normalizePriorityBallotSettings` there — so `priorityBallotDueDays`, the ask window, the ballot floor and `priorityBallotAutoPropagate` vanished on load and the switch disappeared from the pause census. (2) The first cut reused ADR-0143's `priorityAutoPropagate`; one pause would then have silenced **two** engines. The ballot engine now owns `priorityBallotAutoPropagate`; ADR-0143's switch is untouched, and its "read but not carried" defect stays recorded in `CONVENTION_SWITCH_DEFECTS`. (3) The reader claimed a Schulze **order** for a balanced 3-cycle in which every pair ties; it now reports `strict: false` and an empty order — no leader where none exists. (4) Withdrawn rounds were charged to coverage, though they can never be answered; the ballot stays on its row as history and the round owes nothing. (5) The "name which of your two lines" branch was unreachable — the matrix engine refuses a second line to the same manager — so it was deleted instead of shipped as dead code. Two test-fixture traps were also found and are now pinned in comments: a `reportingLines.kind` outside `MATRIX_LINE_KINDS` drops the line as a matrix defect, and a seat must have its own span or be read-only.

**Verification — one focused pass.** `tests/app/priority-ranking.test.mjs` **23/23 PASS** on a hand-computed fixture: R1 balanced 3-cycle (no Condorcet, Schwartz = all three, no strict beatpath, Kemeny tied 3@5), R2 unique Condorcet `p1` and unique Kemeny `p1>p2>p4` = 7, R3 overdue/insufficient, R4 single-line inert, R5 withdrawn; coverage 7 answered of 10 live frozen lines = 70.0%, Wilson 39.7–89.2; spine integration through the real store with `delegations` asks and idempotent re-scan; JSDOM panel render. Adjacent pins in the same pass: `pause-permits` 14/14, `core` 15/15, `standard-work` 25/25, `board-room` 45/45, `csv` 5/5, `app` 33/33, `interface-alignment` + `aar-corroboration` 39/39, `matrix-interface` + `autopilot` + `assessment-reconciliation` + `decision-tempo` 81/81, `attention-market` 31/31, `matrix-line-seat` + `dependency-cycles` green. Repo-wide pin churn: v150→v151 in 159 suites, migration tails in 99, CSV 143→144 in 57, spine 120→121 in 3. `verify:standalone` 7/7 (`12,512,536` bytes · `809` modules, ceiling raised to 12,650,000); `check:boundary` 811 app modules / 242 suites / 23 doc checks; `check:links` 1,133 links across 308 markdown files; `eslint` unavailable in this sandbox's jsdom-only `node_modules` **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Higgsfield Asset Mandate.** `list_connector_tools('higgsfield')` → `unsupported` (no app connected). The concrete blind spot: `matrixInterface.js`'s pair accumulator iterates only multi-line allocations, so agreement-only pairs never appear at all (the idle case). NORTH_STAR.md doctrine verified; ROADMAP and this log read at orientation.

**Gap discovery & selection (Step 2–3).** Four NEW gaps logged to ROADMAP Planned: organisational ambidexterity (March 1991 special issue; O'Reilly & Tushman), Argyris ladder of inference / espoused theory vs theory-in-use / defensive routines, spiral of silence (Noelle-Neumann), Wardley evolution stages. Repository-wide absence of each **[UNVERIFIED]** beyond the targeted searches (0 hits for `ambidexterity|exploration.exploitation`, `ladder of inference|espoused theory|Argyris`, `spiral of silence|Noelle-Neumann`, `Wardley|evolution stage`).

**Benchmark (Step 3.5).** Structure101 / Lattix DSM, Cambridge Advanced Modeller, Orgvue, Team Topologies tooling and Sosa's own manual alignment-matrix practice: each covers part (DSM partitioning, org modelling, capacity planning) and none joins a typed-link precedence graph + reporting lines + recorded bilateral channels with refusals-not-imputation and governed idempotent asks. Matched on the DSM side by reusing the exact dependencyCycle lexicon/directions/partitioning discipline; exceeded on the governance side (governed asks, self-closing, pause-governed). **[BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED]** against commercial DSM org-design consulting, which does this by hand and is not inspectable.

**Built (one item, end-to-end).** (1) Reader `interfaceAlignment.js` + closed vocabularies in `interfaceAlignmentRegistry.js` + `interfaceAlignmentSchema.js` (bounded settings: `interfaceAlignmentWindowDays` 180 [30–730], `interfaceAlignmentAskDueDays`, `interfaceAlignmentAutoPropagate`; `ifa1:` dual-FNV signature): pair universe = cross-holder-dependency ∪ agreement-act ∪ shared-active-forum (shared-people-only pairs counted as context, never rows); arc attribution identical to RULE-CYC-01 (single manager via `lineReach`, else `unattributed-endpoint` refusal — never charged to a home line); 7 pair verdicts in closed rank order (0 `unattended-interface` / 1 `channel-not-live` / 2 `idle-channel` / 3 `forum-without-interface` / 4 `channel-without-interface` / 5 `standing` / 6 `attended`) with the two failure modes never collapsed and out-of-window dependencies printed beside the idle finding; 8 channel states derived from the ADR-0162 acts by the register's OWN channel question (ADR-0162 stays authoritative, surfaced as `agreementStateDetail`); `matrixJointApex` escalation (unique / leader-held / ambiguous / unattributed / off-roster / not-applicable); three separate claims (`countsReconcile`, `figuresComplete`, `interfacesClaim` refused on unattributed arcs / matrix defects / bounds with the reason printed). (2) `RULE-OIA-01` (`interfaceAlignmentRules.js`) on the shared spine (postCommit + propagationContracts 1:1, `writes: ['delegations','links','matrixMarkers']`, `emits: []`): ONE dated ask per finding to the unique joint apex (cap 6/commit, remainder deferred and counted), typed `issues-obligation` + `rolls-up-to` provenance via `ensureEntityLink`, idempotent per `ifa1:` signature, self-closing (`Closed by RULE-OIA-01:`) on clearance or seat move, human `done`/`returned` answers never re-asked, identity-free `matrixMarkers` observations (`kind: 'interface-alignment'`, capped at `MATRIX_LIMITS.markers`) for ambiguous/leader-held/off-roster — nobody substituted; the `interfaceAlignmentAutoPropagate` pause governs EVERY path including explicit scans. (3) Actions `matrixInterfaceAlignmentReport` (pure read, `declare-matrix-line`, seat-scoped `visiblePersonIds`) / `matrixInterfaceAlignmentScan` / `matrixInterfaceAlignmentSetConventions` (out-of-bounds values refused with the bound printed, never clamped); right `govern-interface-alignment` (director+); SAFE_READS + pause-permit registry both lists. (4) Panel `#/matrix?panel=alignment` (`matrixInterfaceAlignmentPanel.js`, the 34th matrix panel): board + pair table + interface table + channel states + joint-apex roll-ups + refusals + scan/conventions + identity-free brief + method card with the generated diagram; manager names redacted outside the seat's visible population (ids shown, never names). (5) CSV scope #142 `matrix-interface-alignment`: one workspace row + one row per pair verdict + one per channel state + one per refusal, counts from the reader's own tallies (never a truncated print list), identity-free (no person, manager, pair key, line, record id, title, note or record date). (6) Asset `app/assets/interface-alignment-matrix.jpg` (conceptual method diagram, labelled not-workspace-data).

**Defects found & fixed during the build.** (1) The first contract purpose string over-escaped `\'` and broke `propagationContracts.js` parsing — re-escaped to the repository's `\'` convention (`node --check` clean). (2) The reader's first draft read `link.from.type`/`link.to.type` — normalized links are FLAT (`sourceType`/`sourceId`/`targetType`/`targetId`), so every endpoint resolved empty; rewritten to the flat shape `dependencyCycle.js` reads. (3) Registry `standing` detail claimed "with or without a declared dependency" while the rank order reads a dependency on a live channel as `attended`; detail corrected to match the printed precedence. (4) `verdictCounts`/`channelStateCounts` are closed maps carrying every key at 0 — the first test draft's deep-equal against a sparse object was wrong about the reader, not the reader wrong about the fixture.

**Verification — one focused pass (new item + adjacent breakage only).** `tests/app/interface-alignment.test.mjs` — 16/16 on a HAND-COMPUTED fixture (counts pinned in comments before the reader existed): base a1..a5 board (5 precedence arcs = 2 cross (1 in-window / 1 out) + 2 same + 1 unattributed; pair `nora|prod` depsN 2, flowsBA 2, sharedPeopleN 1, channel `none` → `unattended-interface`; `sharedPeopleOnlyPairsN` 1 via zoe; escalation unique `ceo`; `interfacesClaim` false citing 1 arc; `countsReconcile` true); idle case (matched bilateral agreement on `nora|ops` + out-of-window a6 → `idle-channel` with `depsOutOfWindowN` 1 printed beside); five dark channels (one-sided / contested-terms / expired-agreement / future-agreement / forum-unavailable via a retired forum named in the terms) each `channel-not-live`; `attended` (shared active forum), `standing` (live agreement + shared zoe + no dependency), `forum-without-interface` and `channel-without-interface` context rows; diamond apex `m1|m2` → minimal ancestors {ann1, ann2} → `ambiguous` + one identity-free marker + zero asks; CSV identity-free (banned-word sweep over every value) and equal to the board's tallies; RULE-OIA-01 ask to `ceo`, idempotent second run, self-close `Closed by RULE-OIA-01:`, pause = zero effects; panel smoke (prints the reader's figures, no `undefined`/`NaN`/`[object`). Adjacent: `csv` + `board-room` (spine 119, 1:1 contracts) + `dependency-cycles` + `aar-corroboration` = 111/111; `matrix-interface` + `matrix-governance` + `matrix-coordination` + `pause-permits` + `allocation` + `change-saturation` + `change-impact-review` = 100/100 (fixing the panel-count pin 33→34 in `change-saturation.test.mjs` and the repo-wide `CSV_EXPORTS.length` pin 141→142 across 54 files); `bench-evidence` + `app` = 49/49. **Total: 260 tests / 14 suites / 0 failures.** `npm run verify:standalone` 7/7: `LeadOS-standalone.html` = 12,218,247 bytes, under the 12,250,000 ceiling by 31,753 (the 700px/q34 re-encode of the new diagram is 19,881 bytes; the first 108 KB encode blew the ceiling by 86 KB and was re-encoded).

**Higgsfield Asset Mandate.** `list_connector_tools('higgsfield')` → `unsupported` (Session 255 precedent repeated). The method diagram was generated with the Arena image tool (Session 252 precedent) and re-encoded for the standalone ceiling — reported as a **blocker**: no Higgsfield-quality real asset pipeline this session.

**Unverified / limits (standing).** Full `npm test` (240 files) **[UNVERIFIED]** — the focused pass above is the whole of this session's verification; real-browser / assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**; authenticated identity / tenant isolation **[UNVERIFIED]** (the seat is a declared local role). Register-specific limits: the pair universe sees only DECLARED precedence relations (the excluded relations are counted with reasons); a shared person who works across both spans but holds no line in one is read from active lines as of today (the as-of matrix reader remains blocked); `channel-without-interface` and `forum-without-interface` rows are printed as context and the idle claim stays scoped to active agreements; the export's `claim_reason` is the only free-text column and carries refusal reasons only.

## 2026-11-13 — Session 256: Cross-line after-action corroboration & lesson-adoption parity (ADR-0188, Schema v149, RULE-AAC-01, CSV #141)

**Doctrine / scope.** Frontline / mid-manager / director PRIMARY × **matrix and single-chain**. Took the Planned gap Session 255 recorded, because the prior three sessions were matrix-primary director/VP work and the roadmap's lowest-coverage altitude band was the line manager who actually signs an AAR. Duty: when one after-action review is written inside one line but its lessons or follow-ups affect another line's people, the counterpart line holder must be asked, in the first person, whether they corroborate it — and every lesson it recorded must be traceable to adoption or reasoned refusal in each line it touches. Nothing here scores a person, and nothing here treats silence as agreement.

**Gap discovery & selection (Steps 2–3).** `ROADMAP.md` Planned item (2) from Session 255 recorded that `app/core/domain/outcomeLearning.js` has zero references to `reportingLines`, `buildMatrix` or `matrixJointApex`. Re-verified by targeted search; the AAR ledger of record (`app/core/domain/learning.js`: `retros`, `normalizeRetro`, `buildLearningReport`, `learningRule`) was read and left **read-only** for this feature. `RULE-AAC-01` and `aarCorroboration*` were confirmed free (no `outcomeLearning.js` extension, no `RULE-AAR-*` collision).

**Benchmark (Step 3.5).** Comparators: generic retrospective/AAR tooling (TeamRetro, EasyRetro, Miro AAR templates), knowledge-management systems with lesson registers (SharePoint lessons-learned lists, Confluence), and post-incident review products (PagerDuty, Blameless, Jeli). All of them record an AAR and a follow-up action; **none of them models who had to be asked**. Their unit is the document and the action item; the missing dimension is that a lesson affecting line B is authored and signed only by line A, so "lesson captured" and "lesson adopted where it applies" are indistinguishable. LeadOS clears and exceeds that bar: the unit is a dated request × one **line holder** × one current first-person answer over a frozen participant/line snapshot; an answer from anyone else is refused; a holder carrying several participants is asked once, not once per person (`basisN` prints how many people and lines the answer rests on); an *observation* is stored but never counted as corroboration; a single-line review is excluded rather than counted as satisfied; and lesson parity (`adopted | refused | not-asked | unattributable`) never rewrites the lesson's own `state`. Governance exceeds these tools too: bounded asks on the shared spine, addressed to the holder who owes the answer rather than an ancestor, identity-free markers, self-closing and idempotent, pausable through a registered permit switch. **`[BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED]`** for parity with licensed enterprise GRC lesson-management suites, which were not evaluated hands-on in this environment.

**Built (Schema v148 → v149, three EMPTY collections, no back-fill, no inferred corroboration).**
- `app/core/domain/aarCorroborationRegistry.js` — leaf module (imports nothing): `ADR-0188`, engine `aar-corroboration-2026.11-v1`, `RULE-AAC-01`, `govern-aar-corroboration`, events `learning.aar.corroboration.{requested,responded,adopted,scan,conventions}`, four outcomes, twelve request states, four adoption states, five parity verdicts, counting outcomes `['corroborated','partly']`, withdrawn reasons, `AAR_CORROBORATION_ANSWERS_NOTE`, CSV #141 columns, conventions (`aarCorroborationMinLines` 3, `aarCorroborationAdoptWarnDays` 21, ask cap 8).
- `aarCorroborationSchema.js` — fail-closed normalizers, `normalizeSnapshot({reviewId, holderId, personId})` freezing the holder.
- `aarCorroboration.js` — one pure reader `buildAarCorroborationReport` exposing `matrix`, `aarCorroborationBriefText`, `aarCorroborationCsvRows`, `aarCorroborationSnapshot`; disclosure ladder applied **here**, not in the view.
- `aarCorroborationRules.js` (`RULE-AAC-01`) and `aarCorroborationActions.js` (request / answer / adoption actions), wired into `app/actions.js`, `accessGateway.js`, `seatRegistry.js`, `csv.js`, `postCommit.js`, `propagationContracts.js`, `pausePermitRegistry.js`, `schema.js`.
- `app/views/learningCrossLinePanel.js` + a 5th `#/learning` tab `cross-line` dispatched by `?panel=cross-line`.
- `app/assets/aar-corroboration-parity.jpg` (1856×576, 80,893 bytes) — generated method diagram, inlined into the offline artifact by the folder-derived asset list.

**Four defects this build found in its own code, all fixed.** (1) An AAR with **no lesson** was refused as `no-lesson-under-review`, which suppressed every account-level corroboration on a lesson-free review; the refusal now fires only for a request that *names* a lesson the review does not carry. (2) The rule classified a holder who is also the joint apex as "self-addressed" and merely recorded it — leaving the one person who owed the answer unasked; the ask now goes to that holder, and escalation to an ancestor who does not hold the line is explicitly refused as the substitution the register exists to prevent. (3) A **paused** register still filed asks on an explicit scan event; the pause now governs every path, so an automation switch means what it says. (4) `aarCorroborationSnapshot` accepted a requested holder who held no line over any participant and silently gave them the home line; it now refuses with `off-seat`.

**Verification — one focused pass.** `tests/app/aar-corroboration.test.mjs` **23/23 PASS** (hand-computed denominator, one-ask-per-holder, observation ≠ corroboration, stale-on-edit, single-line exclusion, rule idempotency/self-close/pause/no-roster, action refusals end to end through the store, adoption gating, CSV #141 identity-free, withheld-blank-not-zero, disclosure ladder, panel render against the real reader, signature round-trip, `MIGRATIONS[148]`, snapshot refusals). Adjacent suites in the same pass: `after-action` 21/21, `board-room` 45/45, `pause-permits` 14/14, plus `csv`, `change-saturation`, `change-impact-review` green. Repo-wide pin churn applied once: `SCHEMA_VERSION` 148→149, `CSV_EXPORTS.length` 140→141, migration tail `148 → 149`, `DEFAULT_POST_COMMIT_RULES.length` 117→118.

## 2026-11-13 — Session 255: Dependency feedback-cycle tear-sensitivity analysis & governed post-commit escalation (ADR-0187, Schema v148 unchanged, RULE-CYC-01, CSV #137)

**Doctrine / scope.** Director / VP PRIMARY × **both matrix and single-chain** (Sessions 251–254 were all matrix-primary; single-chain workspaces are served as first-class operators here because a single-branch feedback loop has a provable sole line manager who can sequence it directly, while cross-line loops route to their unique `matrixJointApex`). Duty: tell an accountable leader whether a coupled feedback loop can be uncoupled by re-sequencing one declared dependency or requires multi-arc intervention — without ever auto-tearing an edge or choosing which commitment yields — and place one bounded sequencing ask on the shared post-commit spine.

**Gap discovery & selection (Steps 2–3).** Audited recent sessions 248–254 and `ROADMAP.md` In Progress / Planned: Session 251 (`ADR-0183`) shipped the read-only DSM SCC partitioning slice on `#/matrix?panel=cycles` and explicitly left tearing sensitivity and a post-commit remediation rule (`RULE-*`) In Progress. Targeted search of `app/` confirmed `0` files for `dependencyCycleRules` / `RULE-CYC` / `TearSensitivity`. Also ran proactive Step 3 discovery and recorded two verified `Planned — Not Started` gaps in `ROADMAP.md`: (1) **Product–organisation interface alignment matrix (Sosa, Eppinger & Rowles 2004; Galbraith 1974)** (`0` hits in `app/` and `ROADMAP.md`); and (2) **Cross-line after-action review (AAR) corroboration & lesson-adoption parity** (`outcomeLearning.js` has `0` references to `reportingLines|buildMatrix|matrixJointApex`). Repository-wide absence beyond targeted searches remains **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Benchmark (Step 3.5).** Specialized DSM suites (Steward 1981; Eppinger & Browning 2012; Lattix; Cambridge Advanced Modeller) and portfolio tools (Jira Advanced Roadmaps, Planview) detect cycles and propose heuristic tears. LeadOS clears and exceeds the single-arc sensitivity and governance bar by computing an **exact counterfactual single-pair tear census** (`acyclic` / `reduces-core` / `core-persists` over distinct directed pairs `from>to`, brute-force verified across all 512 directed 3-node graphs including self-loops, refusing above `maxTearArcsPerBlock = 64` with `null` rather than heuristic sampling), refusing to pick a "winning" edge, and routing `RULE-CYC-01` asks through both single-chain line holders and `matrixJointApex` while recording identity-free `matrixMarkers` observations on diamond ties, `leader-held`, `unattributed` and `off-roster` loops. Multi-arc minimum feedback-arc-set optimization, numerical DSMs, clustering, simulation and LLM-assisted discovery remain In Progress (**`[BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED]`** against commercial DSM suites; `higgsfield` connector checked via `list_connector_tools` and confirmed `unsupported` in this environment).

**Built (Schema v148 unchanged — zero new collections, zero migrations).**
- `app/core/domain/dependencyCycleRegistry.js` — upgraded engine `dependency-cycle-2026.11-v2`, `ADR-0187`, `RULE-CYC-01`, `govern-dependency-cycles`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_EVENTS`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_TEAR_STATES`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_TEAR_OUTCOMES`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_ARC_SPAN_STATES`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_ESCALATION_STATES`, `DEPENDENCY_CYCLE_ASK_STATES`, conventions (`dependencyCycleAskDueDays` 1–30 default 7, `dependencyCycleAutoPropagate` default `true`), bounds (`maxTearArcsPerBlock: 64`, `asksPerCommit: 6`), and extended CSV #137 columns.
- `app/core/domain/dependencyCycleSchema.js` — `normalizeDependencyCycleSettings` (wired into `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` and `normalizeSettings` in `app/core/schema.js`), deterministic 64-bit FNV-1a `dependencyCycleSignature` (`cyc1:<size>:<16-hex>`), and `issues-obligation` summary parser/formatter.
- `app/core/domain/dependencyCycle.js` — `dependencyCycleTearSensitivity`, `indexDependencyCycleAsks`, per-block `signature`, `tearing` and `escalation`, seat-scoped `visiblePersonIds` redaction (`withheld outside seat span`), top-level `report.tearing` + `report.asks` + `report.settings`, and extended `dependencyCycleCsvRows` / `dependencyCycleBriefText`.
- `app/core/domain/dependencyCycleRules.js` — `RULE-CYC-01` (`dependencyCycleRule`), registered on `postCommit.js` immediately before `seatRule` and `sealRule` (`117` rules / `117` `PROPAGATION_CONTRACTS`; `board-room.test.mjs` tripwire updated `116 → 117`), registered on `PAUSE_PERMIT_MATRIX_REACH_SWITCHES` / `PAUSE_PERMIT_REACH_RULES`.
- `app/core/domain/matrixActions.js`, `seatRegistry.js`, `accessGateway.js`, `app/views/matrixDependencyCyclePanel.js` — rank-3+ `matrixDependencyCycleScan` and fail-closed `matrixDependencyCycleSetConventions` (`govern-dependency-cycles`), plus counterfactual tear table and governed escalation/convention controls in `#/matrix?panel=cycles` (retaining `app/assets/dependency-cycle-dsm.jpg` embedded in `LeadOS-standalone.html`).

**Verification.**
- `node --test tests/app/dependency-cycles.test.mjs` → **22/22 PASS** (all 14 Session-251 tests + 8 ADR-0187 suites).
- Cross-cutting suites: `board-room.test.mjs`, `convention-persistence.test.mjs`, `pause-permits.test.mjs` → **68/68 PASS**; `standalone.test.mjs` → **7/7 PASS** (`LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuilt: `11,928,631` bytes · `786` source modules · Schema v148).
- `npm run lint`, `npm run check:boundary` (`788` app ES modules, `238` test suites, `23` documentation checks, `0` violations), `npm run check:links` (`1,120` internal links across `304` markdown files) and `git diff --check` PASS (`npm run typecheck` intentionally refuses on the plain-JS canonical tree per `scripts/typecheck-gate.mjs`). Full 238-suite `npm test` and real-browser assistive-technology walkthroughs remain **[UNVERIFIED]**.

## 2026-11-13 — Session 254: Cross-line change saturation & absorptive-capacity ledger (ADR-0186, Schema v148, RULE-CSA-01, CSV #140)

**Doctrine / scope.** Frontline, mid-manager, director, VP and CEO × **matrix** (single-chain fully served; the cross-line claim stays inert until a second line declares on the same person). Duty: let the lines state what a change costs a person, and refuse to turn that into a verdict nobody declared. Matrix was non-negotiable: this is the case no single-chain screen can see — three lines each approve their own change and nobody sums them.

**Gap discovery (Step 3).** Sweep of `ROADMAP.md` Planned + `grep -rniE "saturation|absorptive" app/`: the item existed only as a Planned line (Session 251) with 0 files in `app/`, and the closest neighbours — `changes` impact rows, ADR-0185's per-line change-impact review, ADR-0178's cross-line ask attribution, workforce stock-flow — each read a *different* question. Nothing summed a declared burden across lines against a declared limit. Selected as the item; no seeding, no settings card, both deliberate.

**Benchmark (Step 3.5).** The capability is change-load / absorptive-capacity planning: Prosci-style change-saturation heat maps, Huy's change-pacing work, Kanban WIP limits, and portfolio tools (Planview, ServiceNow PCM, Jira Advanced Roadmaps) that print a "who is busy" bar. The benchmark's normal output is a derived busyness score. This register differs on purpose: **nothing is derived from a person's level, rating or history** — every weight is a line holder's dated declaration; **one change charges one weight per day no matter how many lines declared it** (max, never sum, never average); **no figure is compared against capacity nobody declared** — the cell prints `null` with its reason; and disagreement between lines is printed as `contested`, not resolved. We match the benchmark on day-resolution timelines, per-person aggregation, and export-neutral identity handling, and exceed it on refusals (every withheld cell names its reason) and on the disclosure ladder, which no vendor screen in this class applies to its own rows.

**Built.** `changeSaturationRegistry.js` (452 l. — five weight bands with an explicit `notSaid` each, three collection names, bounds 4,000/400/600 acts/ceilings/markers, `demandDays: 730`, `scanDays: 400`, 120/60/120 report rows, the six conventions and their defaults, refusal and state vocabularies, 39 CSV columns, method/framework text, `RULE-CSA-01`, engine `change-saturation-2026.11-v1`), `changeSaturationSchema.js` (409 l. — `normalizeChangeSaturation*` + `validateChangeDemand` / `validateChangeCeiling`, every refusal a nameable key), `changeSaturation.js` (~940 l. — the reader: integer epoch-day sweep, `changeDayWeights` max-per-change, person rows, pool rows, clearance table, ceiling census, candidate list, six-part act partition, withheld cells), `changeSaturationActions.js` (375 l. — declare / withdraw / ceiling declare / retire / setConventions / scan, seat- and line-gated), `changeSaturationRules.js` (323 l. — `RULE-CSA-01` on the shared post-commit spine, appended before `seatRule` with a declared contract: 116 rules / 116 contracts), `matrixChangeSaturationPanel.js` (661 l., 7 cards, 5 dialogs, CSV + brief). Wiring: Schema v147 → v148 (`MIGRATIONS[147]`, three empty collections, `normalizeState` hooks), `app/actions.js`, `postCommit.js`, `propagationContracts.js`, `accessGateway.js` (rights, `SAFE_READS`, `MATRIX_LINE_SCOPED`), `seatRegistry.js` (two rights), `matrixRegistry.js` (33rd panel), `views/matrix.js`, `csv.js` (#140).

**Two design corrections made during the build, not after it.** (1) A rule ask must go to the seat that can actually act: the ask is routed to the **joint apex of the lines that declared the competing demands**, never to the overloaded person and never to one line holder judging a peer. (2) A pool ceiling must be declarable by a manager whose span exists only through declared matrix lines (`poolHasMembers`), otherwise the people carrying project-pool burden could never bound it.

**A read-model privacy hole the tests caught.** The reconstructed reader first returned fully-named rows to a below-floor seat because the disclosure ladder was applied in the view. Fixed in the read model instead: `detailLevel` is applied where the rows are built (`full` / `span` = figures with `withheld at this disclosure level` in place of every name, id, counterpart manager and ceiling subject / `none` = totals and **no rows at all**, because a redacted row must not read as an empty workspace); pool rows no longer carry a member id list; and the panel refuses to render a board the seat cannot read.

**Standalone-build defect found by inspection and fixed.** `scripts/build-standalone.mjs` kept its inline-diagram list as a hardcoded pair, so `app/assets/workforce-stock-flow.jpg` — added by ADR-0184 and referenced by its panel — was **never inlined**, and every `LeadOS-standalone.html` since has shipped a broken image in the workforce panel. The list is now derived from `app/assets/*.jpg`, so a diagram a view references but the build cannot find fails the build, and `tests/app/standalone.test.mjs` enumerates the same folder instead of repeating the old two names.

**Asset.** `app/assets/change-saturation.jpg` (68,418 bytes, 1856×576) generated for this register as a conceptual method diagram (three lines → one person, weight chips, day bars against a dashed ceiling, one day `over ceiling`, one hatched `no ceiling declared`); labelled in-panel as not workspace data, alt text written against the actual bytes, embedded in the standalone file.

**Verification — one focused pass.** `node --test tests/app/change-saturation.test.mjs` → **34/34 PASS**. Adjacent suites in the same run: `standalone` 7/7 (ceiling moved to 12,000,000 with the measured figure in the comment), `csv` 140-export pin, `workforce-stock-flow` **20/20** — its one red test, `17.41 !== 17.38` in the CSV/panel equality, was diagnosed and fixed here: the suite passed `NOW` as `exportWorkspaceCsv`'s third argument, which is an options object, so the export silently read the real wall clock (2026-11-13) while the board was read at the fixture date (2026-11-12) and the trailing-12-month exposure window moved. `tests/app/change-saturation.test.mjs` had inherited the same shape while being written and was corrected to `{ now: NOW }` with a comment, so neither suite depends on the day it runs in. Six other suites still pass a date positionally and are recorded as a Planned item with file:line evidence. `npm run check:boundary` → **786 app ES modules, 238 test suites, 23 documentation checks, 0 violations**. `npm run lint` clean; `npm run check:links` → 1,115 internal links across 303 markdown files, all resolve.

**Full suite — partial, and the reds are not this session's.** The whole `tests/app` run does not fit in this sandbox (the a11y suite alone measured 173 s), so the sweep was scoped to the 164 suites carrying a schema-version or migration-chain pin — the files this session's mechanical bump touched: 12 `not ok` in the first ~1,130 tests before the 1,500 s guard. Each was re-run at the **clean base commit in a separate worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/clean HEAD` + symlinked deps), where 11 of the 12 fail identically: `attention-market` "rule is registered on the shared spine" (its expected trailing-rule list covers 3 entries where the spine now has 29 — every session since ADR-0140 has appended after the market rule), `board-room` "rule on the shared spine" (count tripwire pinned at 94 against a spine of 115 — brought to the true 116 here, which is what the tripwire's own comment asks of a session that adds a rule), `claim-sceptic` "terminal renders six panels" subtest, `coupling` "Schema v61 additivity", `diligence-ledger` registration test, `disclosure-compiler` "every renderer route has a manifest", `dynamics` "demo runs the real rules", `execution-loop` × 3, `followthrough` "delegation actions validate". **The one red that was this session's**: `change-impact-review` "Schema v147 is additive" asserted `CSV_EXPORTS.at(-1)` — that ADR-0185 scope is the *newest* export — which ADR-0186's #140 legitimately invalidated. ## 2026-11-12 — Session 252: the promotion round looked affordable one decision at a time

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with the Matrix Leadership and Bulletproof Reliability mandates; origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on the session branch. Step 0.5 augmentation check: the Visual/Content Integrity and Competitive Benchmark mandates were already binding (added Session 248), so nothing was added. **Altitude: CEO / C-suite PRIMARY**, the rotation Session 251 named; **structure: matrix**, with a single-chain workspace fully served and its per-line reading inert.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** *"We can afford this promotion round"* — said three times in one quarter, about three decisions, by three different people, and never about the pyramid. LeadOS could say who sits where today, price a reduction, read retention risk and succession depth. It could not say that the middle rung is being hollowed out by the promotions that fund it. Nothing modelled the workforce as a **system of stocks and flows**, because the roster stores a level and stores no level history (`levelHistory`, `gradeHistory`, `promotedOn`: 0 files). A rate may not be estimated from snapshots that were never taken.

**Built.** `workforceFlowRegistry.js`, `workforceFlowSchema.js`, `workforceFlow.js`, `workforceFlowRules.js` (`RULE-WFS-01`, on the shared post-commit spine, three bounded findings, apex-owned), `workforceFlowActions.js` (7 actions), `views/matrixWorkforceFlowPanel.js` (`#/matrix?panel=stock-flow`, the 31st matrix panel, nine sections), Schema v145 → v146 (two EMPTY collections), CSV #138 `matrix-workforce-stock-flow`, safe read `workforceFlowReport`, generated method diagram.

**The decision that is the product: reconstruct the denominator, never assume it.** `rate = flows ÷ exposure`. Taking today's headcount × 12 inflates every rate on a rung somebody has already left, and my first implementation did exactly that — it granted both promotions to a senior stock that never stood there. Each person's dated moves are now walked **backwards** from the reading day: an exit ends the walk and proves the rung was held before it, a hire proves nothing earlier exists, a promotion moves the cursor back to its origin, and the oldest open segment runs to the window's first day floored at the person's own start day. Archived people **are** counted inside the window — they are the exits the model exists to measure — while holding no stock today, so headcount and the exposure population print as two figures and never collapse. A history that does not connect is a printed `roster-contradiction` claiming **no** exposure: the model will not guess which record is right.

**And the output refuses to flatter.** A rate under the declared 12-month floor is blank with its reason, never zero, never averaged; a thin-but-cleared cell with no events prints a **measured zero**, a different statement. Four claims print apart (`observed`/`estimated`/`projected`/`compared`). The quarterly projection carries its integer remainder and prints `roundingDifference`. A gap exists **only** on levels a human declared. A person on two lines is one person in the pyramid and share-weighted hundredths per line via the matrix engine's own `personAllocation`. No identity of any kind reaches the CSV or the brief.

**Step 3.5 benchmark comparison.** The bar is stochastic manpower planning (Bartholomew; Bartholomew, Forbes & McClean), workforce-planning suites (Anaplan, Workday Adaptive, Visier) and HR cohort tools. Those are **stronger on statistical estimation** — maximum-likelihood transition probabilities with confidence intervals over large populations, recruitment-policy optimisation — and LeadOS neither does that nor claims it. What none of them do: refuse a thin cell instead of smoothing a prior, keep the act ledger append-only with corrected acts **kept and marked**, print the four claims apart, and attribute a never-fully-declared matrix split to **nobody**. Meet the estimation standard where it serves these population sizes; exceed it on evidentiary honesty; weaker on large-sample inference — stated on the panel. Real customer workforce datasets not field-tested **[BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED]**.

**Defects found by the first run, fixed in place — not test relaxations.** (1) The per-line reading read `line.effectiveShareH`, which `matrix.lines` never carries (resolution lives on `personAllocation`) — it refused every line and called a complete matrix unresolved. (2) `fail()` in the actions dropped the structured refusal list. (3) `hasMatrix` counted home lines, so every single chain claimed to be a matrix. (4) The seeded demo ledger contradicted the roster in **eight** places — acts dated before the person's own start day, a double exit for one person, arrivals that never joined the rung the walk reached. It is now derived from each person's start day and departure record, with a throw that fails the demo if any person's last move disagrees with their stored level; it reads **0 contradictions**, 4 of 6 rungs estimable, `lead`/`manager` withheld on exposure.

**Verified — one focused pass.** `node --test tests/app/workforce-stock-flow.test.mjs` — **20/20 PASS**, with the exposure figures hand-computed to the thousandth of a month (sam's IC 3.9426 + kim's IC 3.9426 = 7.886 mo under the floor; senior 17.384 mo over it) asserted directly rather than snapshotted. Adjacent, re-run only because the scope count moved: `csv` 5/5 at **138 scopes**. Lint PASS on changed files. Docs links PASS (1110 links). The full 233-suite `npm test` was **not** run in this pass — **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Unverified / limits.** Full-suite regression **[UNVERIFIED]**. Real-browser and AT walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. Upstream import retention of the two new collections is not certified. Higgsfield Agent Skills unavailable here; the method diagram was produced with the Arena image tool and is a real rendering, reported as a blocker rather than dressed up as the mandated skill. The demo carries departure rows for two people the roster still lists as active — the ledger deliberately writes no exit for them, because the roster is the authority on who holds stock; that inconsistency is pre-existing and is now logged as a gap.

**In Progress (remainder).** Declared-level correlation; demand-driven hiring from the forecast engine; per-level hire routing; cost per flow; promotion-queue back-pressure; within-level seniority split.

**New gaps discovered (Step 3), added to Planned.** (1) **Retention ↔ roster reconciliation** — proven reachable by this session's own run. (2) **Workforce cost-flow** — a plan that counts people but moves no money. (3) The demand-variance `serializeCsv` DEFECT this session carried has been **fixed** on `main` by the companion Session-252 export repair (PR #284); nothing is carried forward.

**Next:** frontline / mid-manager × matrix (last primary 250) — the Session-250 change-saturation gap, or the new retention/roster reconciliation gap.

## 2026-11-11 — Session 241: a borrowed person finally has a price — Cross-Line Labour Chargeback & Internal Transfer-Price Ledger (Schema v142, ADR-0177, `RULE-TPL-01`, CSV #132, `#/matrix?panel=chargeback`)

**Doctrine / sync / orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership; Bulletproof Reliability) — no augmentation needed. `origin` is `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work ran on `arena/df9babc1-leados` from `main` at `ee9d9cc`.

**Step 3 discovery (before selection).** Targeted search of `app/` and `ROADMAP.md` returned 0 files and 0 lines for `markov`, `manpower`, `transition matrix`, `promotion rate`, `flow rate`, `time in grade` and `cohort surviv` (and, checked again before writing the entry, for `stock-flow`, `pyramid`, `grade ratio` and `attrition rate`; no stored level history — `levelHistory`, `gradeHistory`, `promotedOn` — exists). Added one Planned item: **Workforce stock-flow (Markov manpower) planning model** `[VP / CEO × single-chain and matrix]` (Bartholomew; Forrester / Sterman). Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty.** **CEO / C-suite PRIMARY × matrix** (oldest rung, last primary 237–238): the per-currency workspace statement and its zero-sum identity. Director / VP read their span consolidated with eliminations and govern the scan and conventions; frontline / mid-managers sign their own key and answer the asks; single-chain is inert. Duty: price the labour one line borrows from another and close the period's internal chargeback — or say exactly which share-days cannot be priced and why. Missing tool: no transfer price or chargeback existed anywhere in the app. Plugs into the shared post-commit spine, the matrix DAG (`rolls-up-to`), seat scope, the ADR-0158 share-days, the ADR-0149 home-line history and the CSV registry.

**Built.** Six modules: `transferPriceRegistry.js`, `transferPriceSchema.js`, `transferPrice.js` (pure read model, brief, CSV rows), `transferPriceActions.js` (`transferPriceReport`, `transferPricePropose`, `transferPriceConfirm`, `transferPriceRetire`, `transferPriceScan`, `transferPriceSetConventions`), `transferPriceRules.js` (`RULE-TPL-01`; spine now **110 rules / 110 contracts**), `views/matrixChargebackPanel.js`. Wired into `schema.js` (**v141 → v142**, two empty collections, two settings), `postCommit.js`, `propagationContracts.js`, `accessGateway.js` (`transferPriceReport` safe read; key acts under `agree-transfer-price`, rank 1–5; scan and conventions under `govern-transfer-prices`, rank 3–5), `seatRegistry.js`, `matrixRegistry.js` / `views/matrix.js` (26th matrix panel), `csv.js` (scope **#132**), `actions.js`, `views/settings.js`. Docs: ADR-0177, `docs/adr/INDEX.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `README.md`. `LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuilt; its size ceiling in `standalone.test.mjs` raised from the measured build with a dated comment (density bound unchanged).

**Matrix correctness.** The lender of each day is the home line RECORDED for that day — today's manager is never projected backwards; a person with no history at all is read against today's manager and every such figure is labelled `presumed-current`. The borrower is the holder of the declared additional line; a share-day whose recorded home line is the borrower itself is `same-line` and not chargeable. A seat signs only its own side; the operator view must name the side it scribes, and such keys are stored and counted as scribed. Asks go only to the named key holder or the borrowing manager and roll up to that manager's roster ancestors in the matrix DAG; a leader-held or departed key is observed, never substituted. Spans consolidate along the home-line tree, and an account whose manager also answers outside the span on a declared line is counted on the span row so the convention is visible.

**Verified (one pass).** New suite `tests/app/transfer-price.test.mjs` **11/11 PASS**: registration (v142, empty `141 → 142`, 110/110 spine parity, gateway, seat policy, panel, CSV #132, exact-integer helpers); fail-closed normalizer (a forged second key, a wrong-seat key, an orphan and a malformed act dropped and counted; a departed manager id kept as stored); inert (single-chain gains no row and no refusal; a matrix workspace with no price act reads `unpriced` and is never nagged); a **hand-computed** matrix statement over a 92-day quarter — 5,225 consumed share-days = 2,780 (45 × 20 + 47 × 40, a retune inside the period) + 1,525 (61 × 25) + 920 (92 × 10); partly priced: 3,555 priced / 750 no-agreed-price / 920 awaiting-second-key, EUR 20,555.00 charged and all three line nets withheld; priced in full: EUR 24,305.00 and USD 2,760.00, each netting to zero and never pooled, with line nets +24,305.00 / −7,625.00 / −16,680.00 EUR, and the director span eliminating EUR 7,625.00 and netting +16,680.00; the two-key lifecycle under declared seats (a seat turns only its own key; decline, withdraw and void are appended acts; each refusal prints its reason); `RULE-TPL-01` end to end through the real API (asks the named key holder and the borrower of an unpriced pair, DAG roll-up, idempotent, capped at 8 with the remainder counted, pausable, self-closing, a leader-held key observed); the lender read from the recorded home-line history (a mid-period move splits the credit, an uncovered day is refused, one unknown lender withholds every net); seat scope (a party reads its own lines and its counterparty, a director its span with eliminations, everything else counted and not shown); the panel driven through its **rendered controls** (a manager proposes from its own seat, the counterpart confirms, the operator voids); and the rule's closing behaviour across a quarter rollover (an unanswered Q3 ask stays open when Q4 becomes the last completed quarter, pricing Q3 closes it with the true reason, an open question is never closed and reissued). **Display == stored:** panel cells, the brief and the CSV cells equal the computed board; reads and exports leave the stored state byte-identical; a real reload over the same storage reproduces the statement. Gates: `npm run lint` clean (whole repository, and separately on the 47 changed JS files); `npm run check:boundary` PASS (739 app ES modules, 228 test suites, 23 documentation checks); `npm run check:links` PASS (1,081 internal links across 294 markdown files); `tests/app/standalone.test.mjs` **7/7 PASS** on the rebuilt artifact (11,182,493 bytes · 737 source modules · schema v142).

**Adjacent code — what was actually established.** A schema bump touches every suite. The full `npm test` was **not** run to completion this session (one attempt was stopped unfinished; `main` is known red — Session 240 measured 3,749 tests with 224 failing). What was run instead: (1) the **29 suites that hold a head pin** (`SCHEMA_VERSION`, the migration chain, `CSV_EXPORTS.length`) were run on an untouched archive of `ee9d9cc` and again here after the pins were moved surgically: identical — 28 all-pass, and `matrix-demand-variance` 10 pass / 2 fail on both sides with the same two test names. (2) **112 further suites** that touch the files this session wired but hold no head pin were run here: 16 pass in full and 96 report at least one failing test. All 96 were then run on the base archive and compared by pass / fail count and failing-test name: **identical on all 96 — 175 failing tests on the base, the same 175 here, none added and none removed.** One difference appeared on the way and was explained: `action-bundles` showed one extra failure while `LeadOS-standalone.html` was stale, and after the rebuild it is 18 pass / 1 fail on both sides with the same failing name. (3) After the last edit, six of the closest suites were run once more on the committed tree — `propagation-ledger` (reads every contract), `matrix-demand-variance`, `hiring-lines`, `line-period-allocation`, `structure-history` and `csv` — and are again identical to the base. The repository's CI check (`canonical`: boundary, links, lint, then the full `npm test`) failed on the last three merges to `main` and on PR #272; it is expected to be red on this PR for the same pre-existing reason, and its result was not available when this entry was written.

Apart from the six suites re-run on the committed tree, the pin and adjacent suites ran before, or while, the rule's closing logic and its contract description received their last edits; those edits sit behind the rule's trigger check (`transfer.price.*` events only, emitted by no other suite) or change one description string, and the remaining suites were not re-run after them **[UNVERIFIED]**. Added two Planned items: **Cross-line labour chargeback & internal transfer-price ledger** `[director / VP / CEO × matrix]` (Hirshleifer 1956; Hardin 1968) and **New-hire ramp curve & per-line probation decision gate** `[frontline / mid-manager / director × single-chain and matrix]` (Wright 1936; Bauer et al. 2007). Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty.** **Director / VP PRIMARY × matrix** (oldest rung, last primary 236); frontline / mid-managers answer the asks; CEO / C-suite read the aggregate board; single-chain supported and inert until a plan is declared. Duty: before an offer closes on a shared hire, know which of the managers the person will report to have put their own judgement on the record — and put the gap in front of the manager who owes it.

**Built.** Six modules: `hiringLineRegistry.js`, `hiringLineSchema.js`, `hiringLines.js` (pure read model, brief, CSV rows), `hiringLineActions.js` (`hiringLineReport`, `hiringLineDeclare`, `hiringLineWithdraw`, `hiringLineScan`), `hiringLineRules.js` (`RULE-HLC-01`; spine now **109 rules / 109 contracts**), `views/matrixHiringPanel.js`. Wired into `schema.js` (**v140 → v141**, two empty collections, two settings), `postCommit.js`, `propagationContracts.js`, `accessGateway.js` (`hiringLineReport` safe read; `hiringLineScan` under `govern-hiring-lines`, rank 3–5), `seatRegistry.js`, `matrixRegistry.js` / `views/matrix.js` (24th matrix panel), `csv.js` (scope **#131**), `actions.js`, `views/hiring.js` (two links), `views/settings.js` (one row). ADR-0176 written.

**Matrix correctness.** A line is credited only with its own manager's evidence; span evidence reads `represented`; an interviewer under more than one declared line credits none (`ambiguous`); asks go only to the named manager and roll up to that manager's roster ancestors in the matrix DAG; a departed manager is observed, never substituted; after the hire the plan is compared with the live lines (`realised` / `drifted`).

**Verified (one pass).** New suite `tests/app/hiring-lines.test.mjs` **8/8 PASS**: registration (v141, empty `140 → 141`, 109/109 spine parity, gateway, seat policy, panel, CSV #131); fail-closed normalizer (4 malformed + 1 orphaned act dropped and counted, a departed manager id kept as stored); inert-until-declared (no plan → `undeclared`, rate `null`, rule issues nothing); the full verdict table on a matrix fixture with BOTH partitions asserted against the printed counts (6 candidates = 8 verdict counts; 13 pairs = 5 state counts; a person under both lines credits neither; 2/6 → 333‰ with the truncation flagged); `RULE-HLC-01` end to end through the real API (one ask to the one silent line, due date, three edge kinds, roll-up to exactly the asked manager's roster ancestors, idempotent on rescan, self-closing when the line speaks, candidate and evidence untouched, refusals, withdrawal); per-commit cap with the counted remainder issued by a scan; departed manager observed and never substituted; `realised` vs `drifted`; seat scope (named manager / spanning director / uninvolved peer). **Display == stored:** the rendered panel's eight verdict cells and brief, and the CSV cells, are asserted equal to the computed board; reading and exporting leave the state byte-identical and the revision unchanged; a below-floor rate is a BLANK cell; a real reload from the persisted bytes reproduces the board, the rows, the acts, the markers and the typed edges exactly. Gates: `eslint` on every changed file clean; `npm run check:boundary` PASS (733 modules, 227 suites, 23 doc checks); `npm run check:links` PASS (1,077 links / 293 files); standalone rebuilt (11,090,433 bytes · 731 modules · schema v141) and `standalone.test.mjs` passes.

**Adjacent code — what was actually established.** A schema bump touches every suite, so the full `npm test` was run once on this branch: **3,749 tests, 3,525 pass, 224 fail**. Of the failures, 191 are assertions pinned to a stale head (`141 !== 119` ×101, `131 !== 104` ×27, `141 !== 123`, `141 !== 112`, `109 !== 90`, … and their parent "n subtests failed" rows) that cannot have passed at v140 either — classified pre-existing **by inspection of the message, not by re-running them on the base**. The remaining 32 files were re-run on an untouched checkout of the base commit: **94 failing tests on base, the same 94 here, plus exactly one this session introduced** (`merit-evidence` — a `slice(-3)` pin my version sweep widened to four entries), which was fixed and re-run green. Net: no regression attributable to this change was found; the suite was already red on `main` before this session (a full-suite run is logged as [UNVERIFIED] by every recent session).

**Unverified / limits.** The 191 stale-pin failures were not individually re-run on the base **[UNVERIFIED]**. Real-browser and assistive-technology walkthrough of the new panel and its declare dialog **[UNVERIFIED]** (JSDOM render only; no axe pass was run on this panel). Encrypted-save durability **[UNVERIFIED]**. Declaring and withdrawing a plan is operator-view only; a line held by the workspace leader is refused; `hiringLineAskDueDays` has no control. The register reads what was RECORDED: a conversation that never reached the evidence log reads as silence. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**Standing debt worth a session of its own.** `main` carries ~220 failing tests, almost all stale schema / CSV / rule-count pins left behind by earlier sessions. Until they are swept, "the suite is green" cannot be used as evidence by anyone.

Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **CEO / C-suite & VP / director PRIMARY × matrix and single-chain** (oldest rotation rung after frontline 235 and director/VP 236). **Duty:** maintain an append-only schedule of standing financial signing limits in exact integer cents per seat, currency, expenditure scope class and reporting-line kind; evaluate spending mandates, committed/spent budget lines and priced decided decisions against as-of reporting graphs (`buildMatrix` + `structureHistory`); enforce *nemo dat quod non habet* and cross-line anti-splitting; and escalate breaches to the minimal covering senior principal (`unique` or unique `matrixJointApex`) without blocking commitments or inferring limits from rank or title.

**Built.** Six new modules wired into the canonical application: `app/core/domain/doaScheduleRegistry.js`, `app/core/domain/doaScheduleSchema.js`, `app/core/domain/doaSchedule.js`, `app/core/domain/doaScheduleActions.js` (`doaDeclareLimit`, `doaRetireLimit`, `doaPriceDecision`, `doaScan`, `doaSetConventions`), `app/core/domain/doaScheduleRules.js` (`RULE-DOA-01`, bringing the post-commit spine to **107 rules / 107 contracts**), and `app/views/matrixDoaPanel.js` (`#/matrix?panel=authority`). Registered aggregate CSV scope **#128** (`matrix-doa`).

**Refusals & reconciliation.** Non-operator seats cannot grant or retire their own limits (anti-self-grant), cannot grant outside their downward DAG span, and cannot grant ceilings above their own active limit on `effectiveFrom` (*nemo dat*). Commitments lacking typed amounts, mixing currencies across linked envelopes, carrying mismatched ISO currencies, lacking recorded limits on the commitment date, or landing on unrecorded structure dates are refused with printed reasons — never defaulted to zero or unlimited. Eight exact partition identities back `countsReconcile`, kept distinct from `figuresComplete`; `conformancePermille` is withheld below `testedN >= 3` or over any refusal/unscheduled/FX gap. `RULE-DOA-01` fast-exits in $O(1)$ when `doaSchedule` is empty, closes resolved asks, issues bounded ratification `delegations` (`12/commit`) with `issues-obligation` and `rolls-up-to` edges, and abstains on diamond ties or `above-apex` breaches.

**Doctrine / sync / orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` exists and contains both binding mandates: matrix leadership and bulletproof reliability. `origin` matches `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work is on `arena/934a5ff3-leados`. They separately count aging decisions, line demands, sitting minutes and declared shares, but no reader joins age-weighted demand across distinct lines to declared share-days with explicit line origin and ambiguous-owner refusal. Added **Matrix demand-variance early warning from decision queues** [director / VP / CEO × matrix] to ROADMAP Planned. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **Director / VP PRIMARY × matrix** (next rotation after Session 235; director/VP last primary 233, versus frontline 235 and CEO 234), with single-chain behavior unchanged. The duty: compare the meeting time a line convenes with the capacity share it declared, without mistaking meeting attendance for work delivered.

**Built.** Extended the existing `matrixMeetingLoad.js` / `matrixMeetingLoadPanel.js`: exact manager-specific meeting minutes are compared with the existing canonical `buildLinePeriodAllocation` share-day read. The panel displays each line/person's declared and observed per-mille, delta, minutes and denominator, row refusals and separate reconciliation/completeness claims. CSV #124 appends aggregate parity row/computed/refused/omitted counts plus reconciliation and completeness flags only. No schema move. It plugs into the existing canonical matrix and share-day readers and consumes cadence records written through the shared store/post-commit path; no second hierarchy or sidecar automation engine was introduced. It deliberately does not trigger automatic reallocation or a manager review task: a calendar difference is not itself authority to change commitments.

**Refusals / bounded scope.** A row is refused when the meeting total is incomplete, the share history is unprovable, duplicate declarations name one manager, the line does not cover the full requested period, or total readable meeting time is below the evidence floor. Historical changed-line attribution is not guessed; a trustworthy as-of matrix-line reader remains the prerequisite. No person scoring and no output attribution.

JSDOM cell text equals those computed report values; reloaded persisted data remains byte-identical. ## 2026-11-11 — Session 235: a line was already over its share and nothing acted when the work was taken on — Matrix Line Intake Gate & Share-Day WIP Ceiling (Schema v136, ADR-0171, CSV #126)

**Doctrine, sync and orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` exists at the repo root and contains both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership; Bulletproof Reliability). No doctrine edit was needed. `origin` is `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work is on `arena/ab196133-leados`, branched from `main` at `b86c1fe`.

**Mandatory Step 3 discovery (before selection).** Discovery confirmed: no demand/workload forecasting, no internal-mobility market, no decision queue-aging read, and no intake-time line gate exist in `app/`. The intake gate was already in Planned (ROADMAP line 201, discovered Session 221); it is the selected scope this session.

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **Frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY × matrix** (last primary 232 — the oldest rung by rotation; director / VP 233, CEO / C-suite 234). The duty, in the operator's words: *someone took on work and my line is already full — who said yes, and where is it written?* A line can be over-committed and the workspace would only see it in arrears. Single-chain workspaces are fully supported (one line, one ceiling).

**Built (every new file is imported by the running app).** `lineIntakeRegistry.js` (closed statuses: recorded / over-share / re-routed; closed verdicts: ceiling-unprovable / over-share / exhausted / within-ceiling; refusals; conventions; frameworks; CSV columns); `lineIntake.js` (ceiling = declared share-days swept day-by-day from `shareTimelineFor`/`shareOnDay` over the line's overlap window; refuse-not-guess on unprovable ceilings; gate report with reconciling board counts; identity-free brief; aggregate-only CSV); `matrixRecordIntake`, `matrixRerouteIntake`, `matrixIntakeReport`, `matrixIntakeCandidates` on the existing `declare-matrix-line` seat in `matrixActions.js` (rejects peers, non-roster people, lines out of force, unprovable ceilings; refuses past-ceiling intakes unless explicit human override with printed reason; re-route voids old intake append-only and records new through the same gate); `matrixIntakePanel.js` (`#/matrix?panel=intake` — board, lines, intakes, record, brief, CSV download, conventions); CSV scope #126 (`matrix-intake`) in `csv.js`; `lineIntakes` collection added to Schema v136 with migration 135→136 and fail-closed normalization in `schema.js`; `MATRIX_PANELS` entry `intake` in `matrixRegistry.js`; panel dispatch in `app/views/matrix.js`; `accessGateway.js` rights for the four intake actions. No stored verdict: every ceiling and consumed total is re-derived on read from dated declarations and intake acts.

**The refusals are the product.** An unprovable ceiling (refused line, history cap, days with no declared share) admits nothing — reason printed, not a zero. A past-ceiling intake is refused with exact figures, unless `override: true` with `overrideReason`, in which case the over-share is recorded as a fact. Pre-gate work is not counted and not assumed zero — the ledger starts empty. Re-route keeps funding history append-only.

**Basis.** Kanban WIP limits (limit at admission), Little's Law (throughput × cycle time), Goldratt's constraint logic (declared share is the constraint; breach needs human decision). Same frameworks as ADR-0160, applied at admission.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/line-intake.test.mjs` — **15/15 PASS**, every count hand-worked from one fixture: ceiling = 4600 (92 days × 50 hundredths) for Q4 2026 at shareH 50; intake of 5 person-days within ceiling (consumed 500, headroom 4100); intake past ceiling refused with reason and `field: 'shareDays'`; over-share recorded as fact with human reason; override without reason refused; re-route voids old (`re-routed`) and records new on target line through same gate; board counts reconcile; brief names no person/line/intake; export aggregate-only (no person id, name, manager, intake title, intake date, free text; refused figures BLANK with `figures_complete: false`); JSDOM render prints period, ceiling (46.00) and consumed (5.00) cell-for-cell equal to computed report. Adjacent suites re-run: `sod-register` 22/22, `bench-evidence` 16/16, `csv` 5/5, `review-stewardship` 15/15, `authority-continuity`, `authority-reaffirmation`, `role-clarity`, `assessment-reconciliation`, `structure-history` — all PASS with updated schema/CSV pins (135→136, 124→125). `check:boundary` and `check:links` pass.

**Not verified.** Full `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]** (timed out in session harness; the three `propagation-ledger` / stale-pin failures predate this change on `main` at `d017c38`). ## 2026-11-11 — Session 234: two names were on every decision and nobody had read them against the reporting graph — Cross-Line Segregation-of-Duties Register (Schema v135 unchanged, ADR-0170, RULE-SOD-01, CSV #125)

**Doctrine, sync and orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` exists at the repo root and contains both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership; Bulletproof Reliability). No doctrine edit was needed. `origin` is `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work is on `arena/05463269-leados`, branched from `main` at `d017c38`. The session's first attempt could not reach the workspace at all (a platform fault, since cleared); nothing was read or written in it, and it was reported as not started rather than as a missing doctrine file.

**Mandatory Step 3 discovery (before selection).** A term sweep of `app/core/domain` for control-framework vocabulary found the gap this session then wrote to Planned: a **standing delegation-of-authority schedule**. The first reading — “no approval limit of any kind exists” — was wrong in one respect and is corrected here: `mandateSchema.js` stores a per-mandate `spendCeiling` with a currency (ADR-0078). What does not exist is the authority a seat holds in the first place, so a mandate's ceiling is never checked against its issuer, a decision's Approver is never checked for entitlement, and two lines' limits over one commitment are never reconciled. `ROADMAP.md` had zero hits for “delegation of authority”, “approval limit”, “authority limit”, “signing authority” and “one-over-one”. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **CEO / C-suite PRIMARY × matrix** (last primary 231 — the oldest rung by rotation; director / VP 233, frontline / mid-manager 232), also serving VP / director seats. The duty, in the operator's words: *which of our decided calls had one pair of eyes, and who could have been the independent second hand?* A search of `app/` found no segregation-of-duties or self-approval check of any kind. Single-chain workspaces are fully supported.

**Built (every new file is imported by the running app).** `sodRegisterRegistry.js` (closed vocabulary: three classes, two routes, two bases, two tiers, four record refusals, three structure refusals, four seat states, five conventions, six named frameworks, 36 export columns); `sodRegister.js` (pure reader — `buildSodRegister`, `readHand`, `readDependence`, `readSeatAbove`, the finding and seat sentences, the identity-free brief and the CSV rows); `sodRegisterRules.js` (`RULE-SOD-01`); `app/views/matrixSodPanel.js`. Wired into `postCommit.js` and `propagationContracts.js` (105 rules / 105 contracts), `matrixActions.js` (`matrixSodReview`), `accessGateway.js` (under the existing `declare-matrix-line` right), `csv.js` (scope #125 `matrix-sod`, 124 registered), `matrixRegistry.js` (panel `sod`) and `app/views/matrix.js`. `rightsConcordance.js` now exports the three name-resolution helpers it already had, so the two readers resolve a name identically. No collection, field or migration.

**What it refuses, and why that is the product.** A role that is empty, plural or not exactly one roster person refuses the decision (never a conflict read from a title, never a plural role split). A decision day the structure cannot answer — a home line with history that does not cover it, a person not on the roster that day, or no decision day at all — is `unreadable`, neither a finding nor a pass. A dependence walked over a home line with no recorded history is printed as **presumed**; custody is always presumed. A share is BLANK below 10 tested decisions or over any unreadable member. A hand outside the acting seat is withheld from the read model, name and id.

**Corrections made during the build, stated rather than hidden.** (1) The reader first returned the *id* of a hand whose name it withheld; an id is an identifier, so both are now withheld, after every count is taken. (2) The panel and the registry said recording a review writes “nothing else”. The probe showed the review is a commit, so every other standing rule runs on it — the epistemic rule opened a decision it was already waiting to open. Both texts now say what is true: `RULE-SOD-01` itself writes one marker and one trace.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/sod-register.test.mjs` — **22/22 PASS** on its first run, every expected count worked out by hand before the suite was written and confirmed against a probe (18 decided → 14 tested + 4 refused → 9 conflicted + 3 unreadable + 2 clear; spans 9 / 8 / 6 / 3 / 2). Alongside: `access-audit` 14/14 (renders the new route through the real matrix view under axe), `csv` 5/5, `matrix-coordination` 11/11, `matrix-meeting-load` 13/13, `rights-concordance` 28/28, `bench-evidence` 16/16, `merit-evidence` 14/14, `merit-parity` 25/25, `merit-structure` 17/17, `role-clarity` 18/18. `npm run verify:standalone` 7/7 at 10,809,904 bytes / 702 modules (15,399 bytes per module, down from 15,408; ceiling 10.76 MB → 10.82 MB). ESLint clean on the 12 app files and 9 test files touched. `npm run check:boundary` and `npm run check:links` pass.

**Unverified / limits / failures not caused here.** The full suite was **not** run; an early background run was discarded because files were edited while it was running, so there is no clean full-suite baseline from this session. `propagation-ledger` ran 28 pass / 3 fail, and the same three tests fail identically on an untouched `git archive` export of `main` at `d017c38` (two byte-identity comparisons of the demo workspace across savepoint/collapse strategies, and a stale schema pin of 119 against 135); they were not touched. Also on `main` before any edit, and likewise left alone: six additive-migration and CSV subtests failing in the first results of that discarded run (before any file had been edited), `operating-cadence` at 15/17, and pins that are stale by inspection — a catalogue count of 104 in `allocation`, `calibrated-allocation` and `capital-portfolio` (it was 123 on `main`), and rule counts of 90 in `diligence-ledger`, `hindsight` and `outcome-learning` and 94 in `board-room` (it was 104 on `main`). Real-browser and assistive-technology behaviour **[UNVERIFIED]**. Seat scoping is verified for one seat (a director). The demo workspace reads 84 of 84 tested decisions as same-hand because its seed records one name in both roles; decisions opened by an automation rule carry the roles that rule pre-filled.

## 2026-11-10 — Session 234 (PR #263, merged Session 238 as Schema v139, ADR-0175, CSV #129): a recurring pattern with no owner recurs by default — Matrix Pattern Repair Register

**Doctrine, sync and orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` exists at the repo root with both binding mandates present. Sync check passed on `arena/6ff403f2-leados` from `d017c38`. No doctrine edit.

**Discovery (Step 3).** The ADR-0146 recurrence lens (Session 198) found repeated structural signatures across distinct decisions but gave them no owner, remedy, review date or post-change check. The SoD register planned for this altitude was not buildable as written: no executor field and no conflict rights exist in the code, so it stays in Planned. A second new gap was found while building: remedy-owner continuity across line exit and reorganisation (Planned).

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **CEO / C-suite PRIMARY × matrix** (last primary 231; rotation due). Single-chain workspaces have no line and therefore no pattern; the panel says so. The duty, in the operator's words: *we keep seeing the same design failure across three decisions — who owns the fix, what did we decide to change, when do we look, and did the decision log move?*

**Built.** A pure read model (`matrixRemedy.js`), a declared vocabulary and refusal set (`matrixRemedyRegistry.js`), a fail-closed normaliser (`matrixRemedySchema.js`), one command (`matrixRemedyActions.js`, `declare-matrix-remedy`, rank 4–5 or full access), the panel (`#/matrix?panel=remedies`), CSV scope #129 (`matrix-remedies`), and one additive collection `matrixRemedies` (Schema v139 on merge, empty on upgrade). The concordance now exports identity-free recurrence signatures so the register and the ADR-0146 lens read one recurrence.

**What is stored.** Only the human act: signature key, owner, remedy text, optional basis, decision date, review date, declaring seat, `status: active`. The verdict, window, count and any “cleared” flag are re-derived on every read.

**Verdict and refusals.** Equal disjoint windows around the remedy date; `pending` before review; `refused` with a printed reason for no baseline, thin review, or a duplicate remedy; `cleared`, `reduced` or `unchanged` otherwise. Owners outside the seat's reach are withheld by name; owners off the roster are shown as defects, never repaired.

**Verified.**
- `tests/app/matrix-remedy.test.mjs` — **18/18 PASS**.
- End-to-end through the real store and `createApi` with `guardApi`: declare → persist → reload → read back shows the stored owner, review date and a printed refusal; a second declaration is refused.
- `rights-concordance` 28/28; `access-audit` PASS; `verify:standalone` 7/7.
- `check:boundary` OK; `check:links` OK; ESLint exit 0 on every changed module.

**Unverified.** Real-browser rendering and screen-reader behaviour of the new panel (the panel is on the `access-audit` route list, which runs axe-core in JSDOM only).
