## 2026-11-10 — Session 233: the meeting hours were already stored and nobody had multiplied them — Matrix Meeting-Load & Coordination-Tax Ledger (Schema v135 unchanged, ADR-0169, CSV #124)

**Doctrine, sync and orientation.** `NORTH_STAR.md` exists at the repo root and explicitly contains both binding mandates — the Matrix Leadership Mandate (multiple reporting lines, no double-counted person, no silently chosen owner) and the Bulletproof Reliability Mandate (no displayed-vs-stored deviation, figures refused with a reason rather than defaulted). No doctrine augmentation was needed. `origin` is `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/fc31a4ac-leados`. Orientation: top of ROADMAP, the last three log entries, the matrix registry/panels, and the cadence domain (`cadenceForums`, `cadenceSessions`).

**Mandatory Step 3 discovery (before selection).** The planned meeting-load item assumed LeadOS had *no* forum record of recurring meeting hours; targeted inspection showed half of that was wrong — `cadenceSessions` has stored `minutes` and `attendeeIds` since Schema v93 (ADR-0087), and `cadenceForums` names the chair. What was missing was the reader and a capture dialog that asked for either field. That correction is recorded in the ADR and in the roadmap rather than quietly inherited. A genuinely NEW gap was also found and added to Planned: the **cross-line segregation-of-duties conflict register** [director / VP / CEO × matrix] — a person holding approve+execute (or custodian+approver) on the same object across two lines, derived from the RAPID rights already declared. Targeted search of `app/core/domain` found no SoD register (one passing mention in `goalCarriageRegistry.js`); repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Altitude / structure / duty selected.** **Director / VP PRIMARY × matrix** (last primary 229 — the oldest rung by rotation; frontline / mid-manager 232, CEO / C-suite 231). The duty, in the operator's words: *how much of my people's week is being consumed by meetings, and how much of it is convened by lines that are not mine?* Single-chain workspaces are fully supported: the same minutes are read, and the panel prints that no line claim is made.

**Built (every new file is imported by the running app).** `matrixMeetingLoadRegistry.js` (five placement classes, three person states with declared precedence, four sitting refusals, seven conventions, six named frameworks, 35 export columns), `matrixMeetingLoad.js` (exact integer person-minutes from `cadenceSessions` × `cadenceForums`, per person / per forum / per chair, inside the canonical `buildMatrix` + `buildSeats` seat scope), `matrixMeetingLoadPanel.js` (`#/matrix?panel=meeting-load`), the panel key in `matrixRegistry.js`, the render hook in `matrix.js`, CSV scope #124 (`matrix-meeting-load`, 123 registered scopes) in `csv.js`, and an **additive capture** on the existing sitting dialog (`views/cadence.js`): minutes actually spent and who was actually in the room. `cadenceActions.js` now records an EXPLICIT empty room as empty — only an absent attendee list falls back to the forum's declared attendance — so the ledger can never charge a sitting to people the recorder removed.

**What it refuses, and why that is the product.** A sitting with no readable duration refuses its attendees' totals (BLANK, “at least N known minutes” printed as a floor); a sitting naming anyone outside the roster is refused whole rather than charged to the people recognised; the home manager's sittings are printed and never charged to a second line; a retired forum keeps its history and a cancelled sitting charges nothing; the rate is BLANK below 12 recorded minutes per person or below 3 measured people per span, over any unreadable sitting or over any refused total, with its reason in its own field and a BLANK (never a zero) in the export. Three figures are never collapsed (`charged` = `printable` + `refusedKnown`) and two claims are kept apart (`minutesReconcile`, `figuresComplete`). One planned class — “chair holds two of the person's lines” — was deliberately NOT built: `buildMatrix` stores at most one line per (person, manager), so the class is unreachable; the registry says so and the match requires exactly one line.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/matrix-meeting-load.test.mjs` — **13/13 PASS**: exact arithmetic (925 charged = 180+135+60+180+90+60+180+40, placements summing to the same total); a reload printing identical figures with reads mutating nothing; the no-duration refusal; the unknown-attendee refusal charging nobody; the one-line-per-manager invariant; home/self/other placement and the retired forum's history; both rate floors with BLANK export cells; the window placing sittings by the day they sat; a single-chain workspace with no line claim; an aggregate-only export whose per-kind rows sum to the workspace row and carry no person, chair, forum title or sitting day; the capture path persisting minutes and an explicitly empty room; and a JSDOM render printing the report's own figures with a refused total as an em dash. Adjacent suites re-run because the CSV register grew and the panel list moved: `csv` 5/5, `line-activity` 11/11, `matrix-coordination` 11/11, `cadence-overdue` 4/4, `cadence-matrix` 5/5, `bench-evidence` + `merit-parity` + `role-clarity` 66/66, `access-audit` **14/14** (the new panel is on the axe sweep). `npm run verify:standalone` **7/7** — 10,754,567 bytes / 698 source modules / v135. `npm run check:boundary` **PASS** (700 app modules, 220 suites, 23 documentation checks); `npm run check:links` **PASS** (1,051 links / 285 files); targeted ESLint **PASS**.

**Unverified / limits.** Real-browser and assistive-technology behaviour **[UNVERIFIED]**. The demo workspace declares no forums, so the panel opens on its declared empty state there — the ledger reads sittings and invents none; every figure in the suite comes from fixtures. Meeting load is only as complete as the sitting log: a meeting nobody logged is invisible, and the panel says so. Seat scoping is verified only through the shared reader (no dedicated withheld-population fixture was written). **Pre-existing and untouched:** `operating-cadence` 15/17 fails on two stale pins (`SCHEMA_VERSION` at 123, CSV count at 109) that already failed at the branch point; the full 220-suite `npm test` was not used as the gate.

**Coverage map / next.** Director / VP primary 233; CEO / C-suite 231; frontline / mid-manager 232. Structure: single-chain 1–188; MATRIX primary 189–198, 200, 203–204, 206, 208–233. ## 2026-11-10 — A tree span can hide four different management interfaces: Matrix Span Fragmentation & Counterpart-Dyad Coordination Ledger (Session 222, Schema v130 unchanged, [ADR-0161](./docs/adr/0161-matrix-span-fragmentation-coordination.md), `RULE-MCO-01`, CSV #121, `#/matrix?panel=coordination`)

Before selecting delivery, the mandatory discovery pass found and added the first Planned item: **Matrix management-interface operating-agreement register [director / VP / CEO × matrix]**. Its repository-wide absence remains **[UNVERIFIED]**; it is deliberately separate from this session’s topology reader.

**Why this item / coverage.** The selected neglected high-altitude duty was director / VP matrix coordination, with a chief/operator aggregate reading. A single tree span can count four people whose second lines all reach one manager exactly like four people whose lines scatter to four managers. The missing engine was a seat-scoped, canonical-graph topology reading; neither a tree-only headcount nor an attributed-hour display could answer it safely. The implementation keeps multiple managers/teams in the same Matrix graph, never picks a primary owner, never mutates a declaration, and never adds manager-span quantities as though they were deduplicated workspace pairs.

**Shipped.** ADR-0161 adds `matrixCoordination.js` / `matrixCoordinationRegistry.js`, the Coordination Matrix panel, identity-free aggregate CSV #121, a safe Matrix report API and an explicit scan action. It reports separate manager-span counterpart counts, directed manager → person → counterpart exposures, unordered manager-pair topology, and exact rational inverse-HHI dispersion. A multi-line allocation with any unreadable active share refuses every holder’s span and withholds workspace topology totals. An active invalid declaration is attached to every live/claimed holder instead of disappearing; an expired/future invalid row does not poison today’s topology; an active defect with no real roster person is printed as unresolved rather than becoming a phantom person. Names follow the existing seat detail ceiling while counts remain visible. The aggregate CSV intentionally does not manufacture a workspace dispersion fraction from incomparable manager-span denominators.

`RULE-MCO-01` is registered immediately after `matrixRule` in the shared post-commit spine and propagation contract. On Matrix lifecycle events (when auto-propagation is on) or a human scan, it writes only one bounded marker-idempotent, identity-free trace for fragmented/refused topology plus the shared event log. It never creates work, selects an owner/counterpart, changes an allocation, creates an agreement, or proposes a reorganisation. Single-chain workspaces remain inert.

Adjacent `matrix-accountability` (24), `line-period-allocation` (20), and `line-activity` (11) tests passed in the joint run. That run exposed one expected catalog maintenance pin in `csv.test`: 119 registered scopes versus the new 120; the pin/comment was updated for CSV #121, then `node --test tests/app/csv.test.mjs` finished **5/5 PASS**. Final combined focused/CSV check finished **16/16 PASS**. `npm run lint` passed; canonical boundary passed (**674** app ES modules, **210** test suites, **23** documentation checks, zero leaks); docs links passed (**1,027** internal links across **279** Markdown files); and `npm run build` rebuilt `LeadOS-standalone.html` at **10,524,146 bytes**, **673** source modules, schema v130 (sha256 prefix `c81b5204f22381a7`).

## 2026-11-10 — A line charged 40% of a person that did nothing all quarter: Matrix Line Activity Reconciliation (Session 221, Schema v130 unchanged, [ADR-0160](./docs/adr/0160-matrix-line-activity.md), CSV #120, `#/matrix?panel=activity`)

**What was built.** ADR-0158 (Session 219) prices what each declared line was CHARGED across a named period. Nothing said what each line DID — so a line declared at 40% of a person that originated no dated act about her for a whole quarter (no brief, no consent answer, no re-affirmation, no settled obligation) read exactly like a line that was being run hard, and a 10% line consuming most of her line-addressed acts read exactly like a 10% line. This session shipped the reconciliation the frontline/mid-manager × matrix rotation needed: one bounded pure read model (`lineActivity.js` + `lineActivityRegistry.js`), one panel (`matrixActivityPanel.js`, `#/matrix?panel=activity`) and one aggregate CSV scope (#120), with **no schema move, no collection, no stored field, no rule and no action** — every figure is derived on read from the live matrix, the dated declarations, ADR-0158's own share-day ledger and four act collections nothing had read together (`onboardingLineBriefs`, `lineConsents`, `lineReaffirmations`, `lineExitRemedies`).

**A structural fact found while building, and what it changed.** The Planned item being delivered compared declared share-days to **completed work items**. Inspecting the record showed that cannot be done honestly: an item names the person, not the line it served, so a person on three lines makes every item she touches reach all three (`matrixWorkReach` reports exactly that). Attributing one item to one line would have been an invented allocation. The observed side is therefore the dated acts a LINE HOLDER originated about the person — exact, because each act carries its manager and its own day. The allocation-to-output item stays Planned and is now recorded as **blocked** on a new prerequisite: line attribution on work records.

**The refusals, each load-bearing.** (1) An act from a manager holding **two** of the person's priced lines is never assigned to one — it is `ambiguous`, and a person dominated by ambiguous acts reads `unattributable-dominant`. (2) An act from a manager whose declared line the period **could not price** refuses the person's whole comparison rather than letting the act drop out to make the arithmetic close. (3) Home-manager acts are counted and never charged to a line (ADR-0158 prices additional lines only; the home pointer moves, ADR-0149). (4) Below the declared floor of 3 attributed acts no share is compared. (5) A sole priced line is `sole-line`, because it necessarily receives everything attributed. (6) Nothing is retuned, no manager is scored, no person is rated: every verdict is about a LINE, and the observed side counts **acts, not hours and not work items**, so a gap is a composition screen — printed as such in the panel, the brief and the export.

**Altitude(s) / structure served.** frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY × matrix (the rotation Sessions 220 and 219 both named); director / VP 219 and CEO 220 read the same figures as a roll-up through the same seat and matrix read model; single-chain supported and inert (a workspace with no second line reads empty with the reason printed).

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/line-activity.test.mjs` — **11/11 PASS**: schema pinned unmoved at v130; single-chain inert end to end; the declared 40/60 split read against 5-of-7 and 2-of-7 attributed acts with observed per-mille summing to exactly 1000 and the printed share-days asserted **equal to ADR-0158's own row** (display == computed); a charged line with no acts reads `silent-line` with the person carrying the worst ladder verdict; home acts counted and never priced as a line; an all-quiet period printing counts and refusing every comparison instead of zeros; an act reaching an unpriced line refusing the person with no share and no gap; three acts from one manager holding two priced lines all `ambiguous`; the allocator exact and returning nothing on a zero denominator; the aggregate-only export round-tripping through `exportWorkspaceCsv`; and a JSDOM render printing both reconciliation claims and the modelled rows while mutating nothing. Adjacent suites re-run because the CSV register and matrix panel list moved: `csv` 5/5 (pin 118 → 119), `matrix-accountability`, `line-period-allocation`, `canonical-boundary` — all PASS; `check-canonical-boundary.mjs` PASS (670 modules, 209 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks).

**Unverified / blockers.** Full 209-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; standalone rebuild and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. Acts are not hours: the reading screens composition, not effort or quality. Management that happens outside LeadOS reads as a silent line, and the screen says so before its numbers. No governed ask is issued from a finding yet (the retune question belongs to the declaration/intake path). Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**Proactive discovery (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **Matrix line intake gate and share-day WIP ceiling** [frontline / mid-manager × matrix]: the app can now SEE a line consuming more than it holds and can do nothing about it before the period closes. Practice's control for that is a WIP limit at admission, and nothing records, when work is taken on, which line funds it or whether that line's share-days are spent. Would record a dated intake act against a line and running period, refuse a breaching intake without a human decision (accept over-share / re-route / retune through the existing spine), and roll breaches up through the matrix. Basis: kanban WIP limits and Little's Law, Goldratt's constraint logic, Ocasio's attention-based view. **Also added:** **line attribution on work records** [all altitudes × matrix] — the prerequisite that makes Session 219's allocation-to-output item buildable at all (a typed line reference or provenance edge recorded at intake). Repository-wide absence for both **[UNVERIFIED]**.

## 2026-11-10 — Closing a drifted portfolio without rewriting history: Frozen Authority Re-Affirmation & Drift Settlement (Session 220, Schema v130, [ADR-0159](./docs/adr/0159-frozen-authority-reaffirmation.md), `RULE-ACR-01`, CSV #117 extended, `#/matrix?panel=continuity`)

**What was built.** ADR-0156 (Session 217) gave the C-suite and board pack a portfolio read over frozen decisions (`merit-cycle`, `decision`, `board-memo`) against the structure of each record's own day, naming `drifted` records and refusing the portfolio claim over an incomplete population — and left every `drifted` verdict permanent forever, even after the current right holder reviewed the file and confirmed it still stands. This session closed the exact gap Session 217 entered into Planned for this rotation: an append-only settlement ledger (`authorityReaffirmations` + bounded `authorityReaffirmationMarkers`, Schema v129 → v130), two explicit human acts (`authorityReaffirmStandBehind`, `authorityReaffirmRefer`), live-matrix authority resolution (`resolveCurrentAuthority`, `canSettleAuthority`), a dual-claim continuity report (`populationClaim` vs `reconciledClaim`), post-commit propagation (`RULE-ACR-01`, 100 rules / 100 contracts), additive CSV #117 columns, and interactive settlement controls on `#/matrix?panel=continuity`.

**The refusals, each load-bearing.** (1) **Original historical verdicts are never overwritten**: `row.verdict` stays `drifted` and `populationClaim` stays `false`; `reconciledClaim` is computed and printed separately and granted only when covered population meets the floor (`>= 3`), zero `unattributed` or `unreadable` records remain, and every `drifted` record is currently `settled` (`stand-behind`). (2) **Unreadable, unattributed, and already-attributable records are refused**: a record with no authority (`unattributed`) or an unresolvable authority/date (`unreadable`) has nothing provable to stand behind and cannot be papered over by attestation. (3) **Subsequent live-matrix drift turns a settlement `stale`**: each act freezes `underAuthorityIds` from today's live matrix (`pay` for merit cycles, `performance` / line authority for decisions and memos); if the live holder set later shifts (`sortedIdsEqual(act.underAuthorityIds, currentAuthority.underAuthorityIds) === false`), the settlement reads `stale` rather than silently carrying to a manager who never signed off. (4) **Contested or multi-principal live authority refuses unilateral peer sign-off**: neither competing line holder may sign off for the other (`canSettleAuthority` requires a common DAG ancestor above all holders), and `RULE-ACR-01` escalates to a unique `matrixJointApex` while refusing to guess on diamond ties (`ambiguousApexN`) or `unowned` rights. (5) **`refer-reopen` records a governed follow-up case (`createCaseRecord`) and `informs` provenance link** without editing the frozen record and without falsely granting `reconciledClaim` (`referred`, not `settled`).

**Altitude(s) / structure served.** CEO / C-suite PRIMARY × matrix (the exact rotation Session 219 named — board-pack continuity and portfolio settlement); director / VP settle multi-principal / contested drift as joint matrix principals; frontline / mid-manager stand behind or refer drifted records on lines whose live right they hold. Single-chain supported and inert on automatic commits (`RULE-ACR-01` abstains unless `hasMatrix` or explicit `authorityContinuityScan`).

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/authority-reaffirmation.test.mjs tests/app/authority-continuity.test.mjs` — **30/30 PASS** (7/7 new ADR-0159 end-to-end tests + 23/23 ADR-0156 tests): v129 → v130 migration to empty ledgers and fail-closed normalizer rejection of malformed settlements; `stand-behind` preserving `verdict === 'drifted'` and `populationClaim === false` while granting `reconciledClaim === true` once complete; refusals on `unreadable`, `unattributed`, `attributable`, short reason, and peer seats; live-matrix home-line move turning an earlier settlement `stale`; contested multi-line authority refusing unilateral line-holder sign-off and accepting the joint matrix principal, plus `refer-reopen` creating a linked follow-up case (`informs`) while keeping `reconciledClaim === false`; `RULE-ACR-01` 1:1 spine/contract parity (`100 = 100`), idempotent reconciliation task issuance, automatic closure on settlement, and refusal to guess on a matrix diamond tie (`ambiguousApexN === 1`); and interactive JSDOM settlement click → real serialized reload (`display == stored`), scoped seat withholding of outside-span settler names (`decidedByWithheld === true`), and identity-free CSV #117 + brief verification. Adjacent suites re-run across schema/CSV/matrix contracts (`core`, `csv`, `assessment-reconciliation`, `line-exit`, `line-period-allocation`, `line-reaffirmation`, `matrix-line-consent`, `merit-parity`, `merit-structure`, `onboarding-matrix`, `review-stewardship`, `role-clarity`, `structure-history`, `altitude-router`, `matrix-accountability`) — **all PASS** (`SCHEMA_VERSION` pin moved `129 → 130` in the 13 schema-pinned suites; stale `CSV_EXPORTS.length` pin `117 → 118` repaired in `merit-parity` and `role-clarity`, zero assertions weakened). `./node_modules/.bin/eslint` on changed files **PASS (0 errors)**; `npm run check:boundary` and `npm run check:links` **PASS**.

**Unverified / blockers.** Full 208-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; `LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuild and real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. Re-affirmation settles structural drift against **today's** live matrix authority (`buildMatrix`) because historical `reportingLines` window projection remains gated by the Session 207 In Progress blocker; an `unreadable` or `unattributed` record still refuses settlement until historical coverage or source attribution is repaired. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**Proactive discovery (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **Matrix Managerial Span Fragmentation & Counterpart-Dyad Coordination Load Ledger** [frontline / mid-manager / director / VP / CEO × matrix]: existing span calibration (`spanCalibration.js`) prices headcount (`N`) on a single reporting tree, treating a manager with 8 reports sharing 1 counterpart line identically to a manager whose 8 reports scatter across 7 distinct secondary lines ($k = 7$ negotiation dyads). Draws on Ocasio (1997) attention-based view, Garicano (2000) knowledge hierarchies, Graicunas (1933) cross-relationship scaling, and Bartlett & Ghoshal (1990) matrix coordination load; would compute exact counterpart-manager count, active cross-line dyad count, and exact rational inverse-HHI counterpart dispersion per seat without person scores or auto-restructuring, refusing incomplete-allocation spans rather than treating undeclared shares as zero load. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

## 2026-11-10 — What the quarter actually consumed, not what today charges: Matrix Line Period-Allocation Ledger (Session 219, Schema v129 unchanged, [ADR-0158](./docs/adr/0158-matrix-line-period-allocation.md), CSV #119, `#/matrix?panel=allocation`)

**What was built.** Every share in the product is one number read for *today*, so a line that ran four months of a quarter at 40% and two at 15% was priced at 15% by every roll-up, the 40% months were charged nowhere, and a mid-quarter retune silently re-priced the whole period the day it happened. This session built the period reading the gap Session 218 named: one bounded PURE read model over records the workspace already stores — each declaration's own window plus the ADR-0140 bounded in-row change history, which nothing had ever read — producing the time-weighted charge per declared line for a NAMED period in exact integer **share-days**, linked at each retune boundary (the time-weighted-return pattern of GIPS/Bacon; the measured-driver logic of activity-based costing, Kaplan & Cooper 1988). Six row states, five person verdicts with declared precedence, printed segments, averages as numerator ÷ denominator arithmetic, a per-day over-commitment floor, one panel and one aggregate CSV scope (#119). No schema move, no collection, no stored field, no rule, no action; nothing derived is stored.

**The refusals, each load-bearing.** A period reaching behind the coverage the retained history can PROVE is **refused with the coverage start printed** — at the cap, five entries cannot distinguish exactly-five changes from nine-with-four-displaced, and today's share is never projected backwards. One undeclared share-day refuses the row, one refused row refuses the person, one refused person refuses the workspace; **no partial sum ever prints as a whole**, and a refused kind total exports BLANK with its reason. A line the engine refused is **never weighted** and the engine's own reason is printed. The **home line's allocation is counted out of scope** — the pointer moves, its as-of reading is ADR-0155's, and weighting it would charge a past period to whoever holds the pointer today. The over-commitment day claim is made **only from a complete sweep** and refused from a partial one — fail-closed in the direction that cannot flatter. The period input is validated or refused (readable days, start ≤ end, a declared 1–731-day bound), **never clamped, never swapped**; the default is the last completed calendar quarter and the report says so. Two identities print as separate claims: the counts add to the rows, and the totals add to the printed total.

**Altitude(s) / structure served.** director / VP PRIMARY × matrix (the reconciliation of a closed period is the director's quarterly duty and the VP's portfolio input); CEO / C-suite read the same board; frontline / mid-manager see their own lines through the same seat-scoped read. Single-chain supported and byte-for-byte inert: no row, no figure, no refusal.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/line-period-allocation.test.mjs` — **20/20 PASS**, including the display-vs-stored check: a real-API `matrixDeclareLine` → `matrixRetuneLine` → weigh round trip lands exactly where the day sweep reads it; every printed row cell was asserted against the **persisted declaration** (history entry and current share) and the recomputed blob after a **real reload**, with reads mutating no byte and bumping no revision; window refusals flow through the whole report; the capped-history refusal names the coverage start and the unprovable day count and the same line computes inside its coverage; the export passed an identity audit over every banned string and reconciles cell-for-cell with the board; and a JSDOM render of the panel printed every modelled cell, both banners and the refused-window case. Adjacent suites re-run because the CSV register, the matrix panel list, the matrix view and the axe route list all moved: **141/141** across `csv`, `merit-structure`, `matrix-accountability`, `matrix-governance`, `line-reaffirmation`, `rights-concordance`, `authority-continuity`, `workspace-navigation`, `core`; `access-audit` **14/14** with the new route in the axe list; `npx eslint` on changed files PASS (0 errors); `npm run check:boundary` PASS (664 modules / 207 suites / 23 doc checks, 0 leaks). The `CSV_EXPORTS.length` pin moved 117 → 118 in `csv` and `merit-structure`; no assertion was relaxed.

**Unverified / blockers.** Full 207-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; standalone rebuild and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. The ledger prices the DECLARED side of a period — an allocation remains a declaration, not a measurement — and the budget/capacity/merit consumers still read today's single share; wiring them through the ledger is recorded In Progress, deliberately not this slice. The over-commitment floor excludes the home line and prints that bound. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**Proactive discovery (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **Matrix allocation-to-output reconciliation — declared share-days against observed work landings** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: the engine already computes per-line work reach (`matrixWorkReach`), but nothing compares a period's declared consumption to what actually landed on each line inside the same window — a 40% line that received one twentieth of the person's completed work is either a lagging declaration or misrouted work, and it is visible nowhere today. Would join the #119 numerators to per-line work counts and closes in the SAME window and REFUSE the comparison where either side is incomplete. Distinct from ADR-0140, ADR-0146 and ADR-0158. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

## 2026-11-10 — A line agreed in 2023 is still charged today: Matrix Line Re-Affirmation (Session 218, Schema v129, [ADR-0157](./docs/adr/0157-matrix-line-reaffirmation.md), `RULE-LRF-01`, CSV #118, `#/matrix?panel=renewal`)

**What was built.** Priya held 40% of Sam since September 2023; her project finished in 2024; the share is still charged to Sam every single day. That is not a bug in the ledger — a declaration stands until a human ends it. It is a bug in the product's *questions*: ADR-0148 made a declared share an ASK with a window, and ADR-0152 read the work an ENDED line left behind, but nothing gave a line a **term of office**, and nothing gave either side a dated way to say *this no longer holds*. This session built that: the manager who holds the line **re-affirms** it at the share it currently carries for a stated term, or **releases** it with a reason; the person whose hours are taken **accepts** or **objects** in their own words. Eight derived line states, ten person verdicts with declared precedence, one rule (`RULE-LRF-01`) and one aggregate CSV scope (#118).

**The refusals, each load-bearing.** A **release does not end the charge**: the declaration stands, the receipt prints `stillCharged` and the percentage, and ending a line remains the governed `matrixEndLine` — an engine that quietly subtracted it would report capacity no declaration supports. An **objection is a finding, not a resolution**: recorded, counted, escalated to the person's HOME line, because ending somebody else's accountability is not the informed party's act. A **lapsed term is never renewed by arithmetic**, and a renewal with no end date is dropped at load. A **changed share is not a re-affirmed share** — the answer stores the share it was given for, so a retune reads `changed-share` with both numbers. A re-affirmation past 100% of a person is refused **with the arithmetic printed**; an undeclared line refuses **both** share totals outright; answers dated in the future are counted and never read as in force. The board's two integers — share covered by a live term, share charged with no live answer — sum to the declared total as a printed **claim**.

**Altitude(s) / structure served.** frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY × matrix (the line holder and the person whose hours are taken are both rung-1 seats); director / VP / CEO read the same board as their roll-up of what anyone has actually agreed to today. Single-chain supported and byte-for-byte inert: no row, no term, no ask, no edge.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/line-reaffirmation.test.mjs` — **19/19 PASS**, including the display-vs-stored check: every printed person, share, term and state cell asserted against the **persisted declaration** and the recomputed blob after a **real reload**, with reads mutating nothing and bumping no revision; a real-API re-affirm → person-accepts → release round trip; the rule's four routing cases through the real `matrixDeclareLine` action and a scan; an identity audit on the export; and a JSDOM render of the panel. Adjacent suites re-run because schema, seat rights, the gateway, the rule list, the panel list and the CSV register all moved: **150/150** across `core`, `csv`, `matrix-line-consent`, `matrix-term-exposure`, `matrix-governance`, `rights-concordance`, `structure-history`, `line-exit`, `review-stewardship`, `onboarding-matrix`, `role-clarity`. `npx eslint` on changed files PASS; `npm run check:boundary` PASS (661 modules / 206 suites / 23 doc checks); rule↔contract parity 99 = 99.

**Unverified / blockers.** Full 206-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; standalone rebuild and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. The register covers DECLARED additional lines only (home-line history is ADR-0149's); an upgraded workspace reads every line `no-term`, which is honest and will look worse before it looks better; a release deliberately leaves the charge in place until a human ends the declaration. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**Proactive discovery (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **Matrix line fractional-period allocation accounting** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: every share in the product is one number read for *today*, so a line that ran four months at 40% then six at 15% is charged 15% in every roll-up and 40% in none, and the budget, capacity and merit readings that consume the share cannot reconcile to the time the person actually spent under each line. Distinct from ADR-0142 (a point-in-time envelope position), ADR-0154/0155 (a pay decision) and this session's term (whether the line continues, not what it consumed). Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

# 2026-11-10 — Session 217: the board asked about the portfolio (Schema v128 unchanged, ADR-0156, CSV #117, `#/matrix?panel=continuity`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` verified present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/9082fb56-leados`. **Altitude: CEO / C-suite PRIMARY** (last primary 215 — the rotation Session 216 named as next, and the longest-unserved matrix-primary rung). **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace gains no row, no ask, no edge, no rule and no export row. The item was the discovery Session 216 entered into Planned for exactly this rotation: the matrix authority continuity register across the whole frozen-decision set.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** "Last month we confirmed the pay cycle was signed by the people who held the pay right. Now confirm the same for our decisions — the pricing change in March, the board pack in June, the arbitration in September. Are they still attributable to the authority that made them?" The board asks the **portfolio** question, not the per-record one, and the product could answer it for exactly ONE decision type: ADR-0155 gave a finalised merit cycle the ability to freeze its own structure and prove it. A decided decision record and a committed board memo could not reproduce their own authority at all, and a March pricing decision naming a CFO who has since left reads *complete and clear* in a pack nobody has re-checked. The failure is silent in the worst direction.

**Measured gap and build.** `grep` over `app/` found `compareFrozenStructure` used only by the merit-parity reader and no cross-module frozen-authority audit. Built: `authorityContinuityRegistry.js` (three frozen decision types, four verdicts, two comparison routes, the declared rights and conventions, six named methods, the CSV contract), `authorityContinuity.js` (the read model, the identity-free brief and the aggregate CSV) and `views/matrixContinuityPanel.js` (Board / Types / Register / Method), wired into `#/matrix?panel=continuity` and registered as CSV scope #117. It reads the frozen decision records the workspace ALREADY stores — a finalised merit cycle, a decided decision record, a committed/frozen/superseded board memo — and compares each record's OWN authority against the authority in force on that record's OWN day, through the SAME as-of structure engine (ADR-0155) over the SAME matrix engine (ADR-0140). The merit type reuses ADR-0155's own `compareFrozenStructure` contract verbatim so the two modules cannot disagree.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) **Two claims, never collapsed** — *covered agreement* (do the records we CAN compare still name the authority in force?) and the *portfolio claim* (may that be asserted of the whole population?) — and the portfolio claim is REFUSED whenever the covered population is below the declared floor of 3, whenever any covered record did not agree, or whenever **any record could not be read**. That third refusal is the load-bearing one: an absence is the one thing a continuity claim must never absorb (population completeness, AICPA AU-C 500). (2) **Four verdicts:** `attributable`, `drifted` (a STRUCTURE MOVED UNDER A DECISION — never that the decision was wrong), `unattributed` (the record names nobody and is never filled in), `unreadable` (counted, printed with its reason, never dropped). (3) **Two comparison routes and the route used is PRINTED:** `subject` (the subject resolved through a typed edge — a link to a `person`, or a link to a `case` whose `ownerId` is the person; a subject is NEVER guessed from the approver's own span) and `span` (no resolvable subject, so the approver must have been a manager with reports on that date — a WEAKER reading, and the row says so). (4) **The rights are the real RAPID vocabulary** — `pay` for a cycle, `performance` for an approval — because an invented right name compares against nothing and every record would read `drifted`, a false alarm in the alarming direction. (5) **Report, never correct**: a drifted record is named; the frozen record is never edited to match today and today's reading is never overwritten to agree. (6) A **board memo names no approver** — its authority is its `ownerNodeId` — and a memo with neither an owner node nor an owner seat reads `unattributed` rather than being assigned one. (7) **The authority NAME is scoped, not just the record:** a record can be in scope because it CONCERNS someone in the seat's span while the authority it names sits outside it — the concordance module withholds exactly that name and this register does the same, so a line manager cannot learn who approved a decision about their person by opening a panel they may open. The verdict and the counts always survive. (8) No person is scored, no manager is invented, no derived figure is stored, no schema bump, no rule, no second hierarchy.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** The register initially compared decision approvals against a right named `approval`, which does not exist in the matrix vocabulary — so every record compared against nothing and read `drifted`; the rights now use the real RAPID vocabulary. The span route read `node.directN`/`node.treeSpanN` on the MATRIX node, which carries the DAG wiring but not the span; the direct-report count lives on the CHAIN node and the route now reads it there. The board-memo reader looked for an `approver` field that board memos do not have; it now reads `ownerNodeId`/`ownerSeatId` as the memo's own authority. The comparison tests were exercising the SPAN route when they meant the SUBJECT route, so they were rewritten with a typed `decision → person` link rather than relaxed. Two test EXPECTATIONS were also wrong and were corrected against the product's actual (correct) semantics rather than the product changed to fit them: a home-line move effective AFTER the cycle does **not** drift the record — a later move cannot unmake the decision — and the genuine drift case is a move recorded after the freeze but effective before the cycle, with the frozen block left byte-identical.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/authority-continuity.test.mjs` — **23/23 PASS**: the register reads every frozen decision type and not only the easy one; a decision is compared against the authority on its OWN day, not today's; a decision whose approver did not hold the line on that day reads `drifted`; a record whose authority cannot be read is COUNTED and refuses the portfolio claim; the claim is refused below the declared floor; a complete population grants it; a record that names no authority is `unattributed` and never filled in; a record whose subject cannot be resolved falls back to the approver's span and PRINTS that it did so; an approver who managed nobody on the record's own day reads `drifted`; the register writes nothing (the state is byte-identical after a read); every printed figure equals the recomputed blob after a **real reload**; the verdict counts and the type buckets reconcile to the population; the export carries no identity; the registered CSV scope carries the same figures as the panel; the brief names nobody; a single-chain workspace with no frozen decisions reads empty rather than broken; an authority **outside the seat's span is withheld** while its verdict and the counts survive; the schema is unchanged; and a JSDOM render prints the computed claim reason and refuses an incomplete population. **The cross-module integration is proven through the REAL API:** a merit cycle finalised through the actual `finalizeMeritCycle` action — which freezes the structure block beside the money — enters the register and is compared through ADR-0155's own `compareFrozenStructure` contract, so the two modules cannot disagree; a home-line move effective AFTER the cycle does **not** drift the record (a later move cannot unmake the decision), and a move recorded AFTER the freeze but effective BEFORE the cycle **does** drift it, with the frozen block left byte-identical. Adjacent suites re-run because the CSV registry, the matrix panel list, the matrix view and the accessibility route list all moved: `merit-structure` + `csv` + `matrix-accountability` + `matrix-governance` **53/53**; the `CSV_EXPORTS.length` pin moved 115 → 116 (the true value after scope #117) in `csv` and `merit-structure` — no assertion was relaxed. `npx eslint` on the changed files **PASS (0 errors)**; `npm run check:boundary` **PASS** (655 app ES modules, 205 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks). `jsdom` was missing from the checkout again and the locked dependencies were installed with `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` before the DOM-backed assertion could run.

**Unverified / limits.** Full 205-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; `LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuild and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough of the new panel **[UNVERIFIED]** — the route `#/matrix?panel=continuity` was added to the axe route list, but the axe run itself was not executed this session. This slice reads the **record's own stored authority**; it does NOT back-fill a `structure` block into the decision log or the board memo, so a pre-existing record of those types is compared against the as-of structure rather than against a frozen block of its own — the comparison is honest, but it is a comparison against the RECORDED history, not against a snapshot the record itself carries. The `span` fallback route is deliberately weaker than the `subject` route and says so on every row that uses it. Upstream import/normalisation retention of the frozen blocks is not certified by this slice. The matrix read-model parity audit and the merit remainder remain In Progress. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **Frozen authority re-affirmation — the human act that closes a drifted record** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: ADR-0156 NAMES a drifted record and refuses a portfolio claim over an incomplete population, but it deliberately does not close one, and nothing in the workspace can: the decision log, the merit cycle and the board memo have no re-affirmation act, so a `drifted` verdict is permanent and the screen keeps alarming about a record a human has already settled. Would introduce ONE dated re-affirmation act per drifted record (the re-affirming seat, the reason, and the authority the record is read under today), a re-read that shows the drift as **settled** with the re-affirmation printed beside it while the ORIGINAL comparison is preserved verbatim, and a refusal to re-affirm a record whose authority cannot be read — there is nothing to re-affirm. Draws on subsequent-event evidence (ISA 560) and the re-attestation literature (Korn/Ferstl 2013 on auditor re-affirmation of prior-period controls). Targeted search found no re-affirmation, re-attestation or clearance act on any frozen decision record in the repository; repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**. Not built this session.

# 2026-11-09 — Session 208: Every line that onboarded this person, or never did (Schema v125, ADR-0150, `RULE-ONB-01`, CSV #112, `#/onboarding?panel=matrix`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` verified present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/b3d8dd4f-leados`. **Altitude: frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY** (last primary 204, the most-neglected matrix-primary rung and the rotation Sessions 206 and 207 both named). **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace gains no case, no row, no ask, no edge and no delegation. The item was the discovery Session 207 entered into Planned for exactly this rotation: dual-line onboarding completeness.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** Sam joins on Monday. Dana is her functional manager and owns the 30-60-90 plan; Priya holds 40% of her for a project that starts the same week. The plan names role clarity, a buddy, the stakeholders to meet, an early win and three check-ins — all of it written by one line about one job. Nothing recorded that Priya's line had any onboarding obligation at all: a project manager had never said, in writing, what Sam owes that project, and Sam had never been told. The plan nevertheless read as the whole story, and a *completed* plan read as a completed onboarding of a person with two managers. The second failure is quieter: `onboardingSummary` counts PLANS, so a matrix person holding two concurrent plans is read as two onboardings of one person — or as though either plan described the whole job.

**Measured gap and build.** `app/core/domain/onboarding.js` carries zero `reportingLines`, matrix or line-scope references; `onboardingFor`/`activeOnboarding` key on `personId` alone. Built: `onboardingMatrixRegistry.js` (eight line states, nine verdicts with declared precedence, two rights, two clamped windows, nine named refusals), `onboardingMatrixSchema.js` (fail-closed normalisers for the new collection and the two conventions), `onboardingMatrix.js` (the read model, the board, the identity-free brief and the aggregate CSV), `onboardingMatrixActions.js` (three dated first-person commands plus a scan), `onboardingMatrixRules.js` (`RULE-ONB-01`) and `views/onboardingMatrixPanel.js` + `ui/onboarding-brief-modals.js` (Board / Case / Method). Schema v125 adds `onboardingLineBriefs` and `onboardingLineBriefMarkers`. It reuses the matrix engine's own line read path (`periodLinesFor`, so one line is one row in every module) rather than a second traversal.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) **Two claims, two figures** — `crossLineComplete` (every line wrote its brief AND the person acknowledged it) and the plan's own `status === 'completed'` are printed side by side; a line that declined makes the case `crossLineSettled`, a weaker word, never `complete`. (2) A line that wrote nothing reads `unbriefed`/`awaiting-brief`, never a zero. (3) The home line with no brief is `presumed` — the plan carries it — and the presumption is printed, never counted as a settled line and never upgraded to evidence. (4) **One person, one case**: two concurrent plans are one case with the duplicate named, `peopleN` is a set of distinct people, and the rule issues NO work for an unresolved duplicate (work addressed to "which of these two plans" could never close). (5) A brief is written only by the seat holding the line; an acknowledgement only by the person — a manager acknowledging for a report is refused, which is the shadow accountability this step exists to prevent. (6) A re-brief is a NEW row that re-opens the acknowledgement; same-day rows are ordered by the order the store recorded them, the only truthful tie-break. (7) An identical brief is refused rather than silently resetting consent. (8) No plan is invented for a multi-line person who has none.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** The line key was re-derived from a field `periodLinesFor` does not return, so every declared line collapsed to the empty key and read `excluded`; it now reads the key the shared reader produced. The home line was evaluating the brief window before the presumption, so a home manager was being chased for a second copy of the plan they already wrote — the presumption now precedes the window test. The duplicate-brief guard sat behind the acknowledged-brief guard, which made it unreachable.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/onboarding-matrix.test.mjs` — **20/20 PASS**: a single-chain workspace is inert end to end and a brief there is refused with its reason; a line writes nothing and reads `unbriefed` thirteen days past the window with the due date printed; the home line reads `presumed` and is never settled; only the line holder may write and the home manager is refused; brief → acknowledgement round-trips through the real storage adapter with display == stored and the brief's words unedited by the acknowledgement; a duplicate brief is refused and an acknowledged brief cannot be overwritten; a re-brief supersedes and re-opens the acknowledgement; a decline needs a reason and is never coverage; two concurrent plans are ONE case counted once and the rule issues nothing against them; a multi-line person with no plan is its own verdict; `RULE-ONB-01` issues exactly one ask to the line holder and one to the PERSON, idempotently under a rescan; the rule and its contract are 1:1 on the spine at 97 and inert on an unrelated event; schema v125 migrates to an EMPTY ledger with clamped windows; a hand-edited file cannot invent a brief, a decline reason or an acknowledgement; every printed figure is recomputed from the blob before and after a real reload and every window line falls in exactly one state; CSV #112 carries no name, manager id, plan id or brief text and its row count equals the computed report; a JSDOM render prints the stored state, the stored window and both completion claims, and an unreadable seat renders as unreadable rather than as an empty workspace. Adjacent suites re-run because the schema, spine, seat registry, gateway, CSV registry and a route all moved: `onboarding` + `onboarding-queue` + `matrix-accountability` + `matrix-line-consent` + `matrix-line-seat` + `reviews` + `review-stewardship` **117/117**; `structure-history` **32/32**; `core` + `csv` **20/20**; `matrix-governance` + `access-audit` + `workspace-navigation` pass; `action-bundles` **18/19**, the single failure being the same pre-existing "Workspace needs repair or migration review before rehearsal" class recorded on the unchanged checkout in Session 203. `npm run lint` **PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings)**; `npm run check:boundary` **PASS** (635 app ES modules, 198 test suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks — after the checker caught a prose phrase its import regex read as a bare module specifier); `npm run check:links` **PASS** (991 links across 267 markdown files).

**Unverified / limits.** Full 198-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**. Standalone build (`LeadOS-standalone.html`) and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. `jsdom` was missing from the checkout again and was installed before the DOM-backed suite could run. Pins this change actually moved were corrected to their true values (schema 124→125, the migration chain, CSV scope 110→111) in `core`, `csv`, `reviews`, `review-stewardship`, `structure-history`, `matrix-line-consent` and `action-bundles`; no assertion was relaxed. Long-standing stale pins in untouched suites (a 104/109/123 drift) remain and are not caused by this change. The home line's presumption is a presumption: LeadOS still does not know who wrote a plan, so the module cannot prove the home line agreed to it and says so. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** Matrix line-exit reconciliation and the orphaned-obligation ledger [frontline / mid-manager / director / VP × matrix]: when a declared line ends, every open obligation addressed to that line's manager — an onboarding brief, a review-input ask, a consent acknowledgement, a longer delegation — keeps its addressee and its roll-up edge while the line that justified it no longer exists, so a director reads work that structurally cannot close and a person is chased about a job they no longer hold. ADR-0148 records an ended DECLARATION and ADR-0149 records a moved HOME line; neither reconciles the WORK. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**. Not built this session.

**Next.** Director / VP × matrix rotation; the as-of MATRIX read remains blocked on the historical home graph and seat-scoped output (Session 207); the matrix read-model parity audit remains In Progress; authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

# 2026-11-09 — Session 206: the org chart gets a history (Schema v124, ADR-0149, `RULE-SHR-01`, CSV #111, `#/structure-history`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin verified as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/7490cc11-leados`. **Altitude: director / VP PRIMARY** (last primary 197 — named as the next rotation by Sessions 203, 204 and 205, and not taken in any of them). **Structure: matrix**; the ordinary single-chain tree is a first-class case and gains no row, no ask and no change.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** "Priya accepted 40% of Sam on the 12th of February. On that day Sam was on Dana's line. Dana is no longer Sam's manager. Which manager was Sam's manager on the day Priya said yes — and is the acceptance I am reading against the structure that existed, or against today's?" `person.managerId` is a pointer with no history, so every stored act that names a manager was silently re-read against the present on every render. That is the gap Sessions 203, 204 and 205 each flagged and none could close, because it needs a new kind of record rather than a new read over the existing one.

**Measured gap and build.** `grep -rin "as-of|asOf|reorganis|managerHistory|lineHistory"` over `app/core` found only per-module `asOf` parameters applied to *today* and ADR-0099's `previousManagers`, which is design-scoped; no roster-wide structure history existed anywhere. Built `structureHistoryRegistry.js` (four stored events, three observation states, four verdicts, four act states, three declared act families, one clamped window), `structureHistorySchema.js` (fail-closed normalizers — a period is dropped, never repaired), `structureHistory.js` (the as-of reader, the diff and the act reconciliation), `structureHistoryActions.js` (the dated move writer, the today-seeding writer, the committed-design import and a scan), `structureHistoryRules.js` (`RULE-SHR-01`) and `views/structureHistory.js`. Schema v124 adds `structureHistory` and `structureHistoryMarkers`; every state, span, verdict and count is derived on read.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) A day the ledger does not cover is `unknown` and every derived figure is **withheld** — never today's roster printed as the past; a workspace upgraded to v124 has no recorded past at all and says so. (2) A partial reading withholds the spans rather than printing a smaller, tidy list over the people it happens to cover. (3) **Drift is reported, never corrected** — nothing is re-pointed, migrated or withdrawn; a human decides what the act means. (4) `unverifiable` is not `consistent`: an act in an unrecorded window is neither confirmed nor contradicted, and treating that window as agreement is the fail-open direction. (5) `appeared`, `disappeared` and `moved` are three different counts, because a joiner is not a mover. (6) The pointer and the ledger move in **one transaction**, so they cannot disagree even for one revision. (7) `structureSeedCurrent` records today and claims nothing about yesterday — the honest escape from the empty state, explicitly not a back-fill.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** (a) An open period always "covered" every later day, so once any baseline existed **every future move was refused** — a reorganisation could never be recorded. Replaced with a `periodConflict` that distinguishes a CLOSED period (history; refuse) from an OPEN one (current state; supersede), and refuses inserting underneath a later period. (b) The act reconciler's first draft used a precedence-broken `.some(...) && .some(...)` expression that silently narrowed the declared-line test; rewritten to a single explicit predicate that also honours the line's own window. (c) A home-line move was refused for a reason the *fixture* caused, and the guard that caused it — a period recorded before the period it describes began — is real protection and was kept. (d) An unused `manager_span_n` CSV column was declared but never populated; removed rather than shipped as a permanently blank column.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/structure-history.test.mjs` — **32/32 PASS**: an uncovered day reads `unrecorded` with every span withheld and the pointer demonstrating the trap; a partial day withholds spans and names the fraction; a fully recorded day's spans reconcile exactly to the observed headcount; a future date is refused; a future start date is knowledge, not ignorance; joined/left/moved are three counts with unrecorded dates `uncomparable`; a no-movement comparison reports no movement rather than failure; a drifted act is named with both managers and a read mutates nothing; an unrecorded window is `unverifiable`, not drift; an undated act is never assumed current; the four buckets are exactly the whole set; the writer moves pointer and ledger together and closes the period the day before; the writer refuses a future date, a no-op and no reason; a peer is refused; a closing seat may record the move; a person cannot move their own line; a closed period is not rewritten; today-seeding is not a back-fill; move → reload → display == stored; `RULE-SHR-01` is on the spine with a 1:1 contract and issues exactly one ask per person, idempotently; an empty-history workspace gains no row, ask or edge; single-chain is fully supported; schema v124 migrates to an EMPTY ledger; the normalizer drops four kinds of bad row; CSV #111 counts equal the computed report and leak no name or act id; a refused figure is blank, never zero; the brief names nobody; overlapping periods are reported, not resolved; `homeLineOn` never falls back to the pointer; and every report figure equals the stored blob recomputed. **Adjacent re-runs green:** `matrix-accountability` **24/24**, `matrix-line-seat` **13/13**, `matrix-line-consent` **18/18**, `review-stewardship` **17/17**. `npx eslint .` PASS; `node scripts/check-canonical-boundary.mjs` PASS (628 app ES modules, 197 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `node scripts/check-docs-links.mjs` PASS (989 links across 266 files).

**Unverified / limits.** The full 197-suite `node --test "tests/app/*.test.mjs"` run exceeded the 1800s command ceiling and is **[UNVERIFIED]**. `jsdom` was absent from the checkout and was installed (`npm install`) before the DOM-backed suites could run — the earlier all-failing matrix suites were an environment gap, not a regression. Standalone build and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. Three pins this change actually moved were updated to their true values (schema 123→124 in two suites, CSV scope count 109→110); no assertion was relaxed. Historical roster membership is still not reconstructed, so a person archived before v124 is absent from every as-of reading rather than shown as `not-on-roster`. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **As-of structure reads for the MATRIX layer itself** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: this session gives the HOME line a history, but `reportingLines` (the declared additional lines) carry `fromISO`/`toISO` and are still filtered against *today*, not against a requested day. A secondary-line acceptance can therefore still be read against a line that had not started yet. Not built this session.

**Next.** The matrix-layer as-of read above; then the standing matrix read-model parity audit (`accessGateway.js` payload helpers, `succession.js`) and authenticated identity + tenant isolation.

# 2026-11-09 — Session 204: a declared share is an ask until two people answer it (Schema v123, ADR-0148, `RULE-LCN-01`, CSV #110)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/0a58e8b9-leados`. **Altitude: frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY** (last primary 203); the register is the duty of the manager a line was handed to and of the person whose hours it takes, and the same board is the director's roll-up of what has actually been agreed. **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace gains no row, no marker, no ask.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** Priya has been given 40% of Sam on my project. She never agreed a delivery with Sam, and nobody has told Sam. The matrix ledger prints 40% as though it were agreed, and every roll-up inherits it. That is shadow accountability — responsibility that exists on a chart and nowhere in the relationship — and it was the failure the register itself created: ADR-0140 recorded a declaration and had no way to record an answer.

**Measured gap and build.** `grep` over `app/core/domain/matrix*.js` found no acceptance, acknowledgement or expiry concept anywhere; the only mention of "accept" in the module's prose was a declaration-time phrase. Built `lineConsentRegistry.js` (events, six states, seven verdicts with declared precedence, three clamped windows, two new rights), `lineConsentSchema.js` (fail-closed normalizers; an acceptance with no expiry or a decline with no reason is DROPPED with a printed warning, never repaired), `lineConsent.js` (the seat-scoped read model, board, identity-free brief, aggregate CSV), `lineConsentActions.js` (accept / decline / acknowledge / scan, every one dated and first-person), `lineConsentRules.js` (`RULE-LCN-01`) and `views/matrixConsentPanel.js`. Schema v123 adds `lineConsents` and `lineConsentMarkers`; stored rows carry the side, the outcome, the day, the seat, the window, **the share that was accepted**, an optional basis and a mandatory decline reason. Every state, lapse, day count, verdict and total is derived on read.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) A declaration is not an acceptance: `unanswered` is its own word and *declared* prints beside *accepted* as a different figure. (2) A refusal is an answer, not a deletion: a declined line stays CHARGED because only the declaring manager or the span above both can end it, and the panel counts those. (3) **A retuned share is not a consented share** — the acceptance stores the number it was given for, so 40% accepted then 90% declared reads `changed`, prints both numbers, drops out of the accepted total and re-asks at the new share (the marker carries the share so the earlier declaration's marker cannot suppress it). (4) An acceptance that would commit more than 100% of a person is refused with `40% + 70% = 110%` in the message; when any share is undeclared the capacity check is refused, not guessed. (5) If any line in scope has no allocation, both share totals are refused with the reason — never a total over a part, never a zero. (6) Only the line's manager may accept or decline; only the person may acknowledge; where the person has no seat (LeadOS has no seated identity for a non-leader) the operator may capture the statement and the row is marked `operator`, never printed as authentication. (7) `RULE-LCN-01` asks the PERSON, not the manager, once an acceptance has stood past its window unacknowledged — the manager answering is not the person being told.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** Seeded acceptance rows had no stored share, so every historical acceptance read `changed` — which showed the state rule was right and the fixture was wrong; the fix was to store the accepted share at write time. The decline and acknowledgement rows omitted `sharePct`, so a reload produced a different object shape than memory while the display matched — fixed by writing the null explicitly. The panel's identity line did not yet count the new `changed` state, so it printed "THE COUNTS DO NOT ADD UP" — exactly the failure it exists to catch, caught.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/matrix-line-consent.test.mjs` — **18/18 PASS**: `unanswered` for a declared share nobody answered; an undeclared allocation refuses both totals and still counts states; the home manager and a peer refused when accepting; accept → acknowledge round-trips through the real storage adapter and survives reload with display == stored; acknowledgement refused before acceptance and refused twice; a manager cannot acknowledge for the person; a decline needs a reason, is stored, and the share stays charged; accepting past one whole person refused with the arithmetic; a lapsed acceptance can be re-accepted; a retune reads `changed` with both numbers and a re-ask at the new share; `RULE-LCN-01` issues exactly one acceptance ask per line idempotently and addresses the acknowledgement ask to the person; rule/contract spine 1:1; schema v123 additive and clamping; a no-expiry acceptance dropped at load; a single-chain workspace inert end to end; CSV aggregate-only with counts equal to the computed report; and a JSDOM render printing the stored state, share and window. Adjacent re-runs green: `core` 15/15, `csv` 5/5, `matrix-accountability` + `review-stewardship` 41/41. `npm run lint` PASS; `npm run check:boundary` PASS (622 app ES modules, 196 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `npm run check:links` PASS (988 links across 266 files).

**Unverified / limits.** Full canonical suite, standalone build and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. `propagation-ledger` shows 3 failures: one migration pin at `SCHEMA_VERSION 119` and two savepoint-equivalence assertions; the savepoint assertion was reproduced on a clean `HEAD` worktree before any edit, so it is pre-existing. Long-standing scope-count pin drift remains in suites pinning 104/108 (pre-existing); the pins this change actually moved (to 109) were updated. Historical roster membership is still not reconstructed. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** Matrix reorganisation reconciliation and as-of reads [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: the register now records who answered, but the workspace stores `person.managerId` as a pointer with no history, so a re-org silently re-points every stored consent and every historical roll-up at a structure that did not exist. Not built this session.

**Next.** Director / VP × matrix rotation; the matrix read-model parity audit remains In Progress (`succession`, `disclosure`, non-matrix gateway payload helpers, tree-only branch partitions). Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

## 2026-11-09 — Session 197: the decision log is audited against the rights the lines declared

**Built / altitude / structure.** Director / VP PRIMARY (ranks 3–4); every altitude reading the matrix report inherits the same concordance, and a single-chain workspace is inert. **Structure: matrix — 197 continues the MATRIX primary rotation** (single-chain 1–188; matrix primary 189–197). The duty served: a director who has just read the matrix's declared rights and the contradiction audit's charters asks the dated reverse about the DECISION LOG — "we recorded that this manager decided this, on this day; did the structure we declared actually give them the authority, and were the people the structure says must be heard recorded as heard?"

**The measured gap.** Session 196's own roadmap named this exact gap as its next discovery, flagged `[UNVERIFIED]` pending a full repository audit. The audit found it real: `decisions` carry DACI names and `decidedOn` dates, the typed provenance graph links decisions to people and cases, the matrix ledger declares per-line windows and six RAPID rights — and no reader joined the two. A declared right no decision ever respects is decoration; a decision made by a manager the structure gave no right to is a bypass that will be re-fought every time, because the next ruling has no precedent to point at.

**Shipped (Schema v121 unchanged — no new collection, node, relation, rule, event, action, setting or schema bump).** `app/core/domain/rightsConcordance.js` + `rightsConcordanceRegistry.js`, wired into the existing `buildMatrixReport` as an eighth panel (`concordance`), the matrix brief's identity-free clause, and CSV scope #108. Every figure is re-derived on every render from records the workspace already writes.

- **The join, per completed decision.** The subject resolves ONLY through typed provenance edges (a link to a person, or to a case whose recorded owner is the person); a decision with no such edge is counted **unscoped and printed, never guessed** into the approver's span. The subject's active lines are re-derived by the real `buildMatrix` AT THE DECISION DATE — a line declared after, or ended before, the decision is excluded by the same engine the rest of the workspace uses.
- **Four structural findings, each about a decision against the declared structure, never a person rating.** `bypass` (decider held no declared right), `contested-decided` (a right two managers hold — duplicate-approval risk), `unresolved-authority` (a right held by nobody — the decision filled the gap), `consult-gap` (an operational-right holder not recorded among the contributors; stewardship holders are not required to be consulted, printed so it can be argued with).
- **Four scope limits, counted and never mapped into a finding.** `unresolved-decider` (name not on the roster, or ambiguous between two), `single-line-subject` (the matrix's own scope is multi-line people), `unscoped-decision`, `dangling-subject`. Every printed count is one of four identities asserted in the suite: `audited = withFindings + clean`, `findings = shown + withheld`, `queue = shown + remainder`, `completed = audited + unscoped + singleLine`.
- **Seat-scoped like the matrix report; nothing written.** The seat and scope set are injected by `buildMatrixReport`; findings outside the seat's span are withheld and counted, never shown. The state is byte-identical after a read (pinned by test).

**A pre-existing shipped defect found by driving the live API, fixed in place.** The matrix module gates every command — reads included — on the declared right `declare-matrix-line`, but that right was never registered in `ACTION_RIGHTS`, the seat spine's single source of truth. The live API therefore refused `matrixReport`, `matrixBriefText`, `matrixCsvRows` and `matrixCandidates` for EVERY seat, including the undeclared operator view: the whole matrix terminal was unreachable through the shipped application. The right is now registered (rank 1–5, every leadership seat, the same window as `declare-line-mandate`), with the comment recording why an unregistered right fails closed for everybody.

**Verified (one focused pass).** New `rights-concordance` suite **27/27 PASS** (`node --test`): the four identities on a mixed workspace, subject resolution through both typed routes, the unscoped / dangling / ambiguous / unresolved-decider limits, the single-line scope boundary, date-windowed line rights (declared-after, ended-before, presumed home rights), all four findings with their exact negative cases, seat withholding and the full-access seat, the byte-identical no-write check, CSV round-trip equality with no person, name or id in any cell, the inert single-chain workspace, the axe-clean JSDOM panel render, panel registration, and the store-backed API carrying the concordance.

**Two real defects the suite caught, both fixed.** (1) The CSV export indexed the finding rows' kebab-case keys against the counts' camelCase accumulators — only `bypass` matched by coincidence, the other three kinds printed 0, which is precisely the silent zero this product refuses; the mapping is now declared once, with the comment recording that the export must never print a zero for a kind that fired. (2) The missing `declare-matrix-line` registration above, found by driving the real store-backed API rather than by reading the gating code.

**Flagged / blockers.** Findings are statements about decisions against declared structures, never person ratings, and the panel says so where a reader would look for a score. The roster is read as it stands — historical roster state is not reconstructed, and the panel says which windows it used. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. Full canonical suite, standalone build and real-browser AT walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]** — the focused pass is the verification. No blocker.
