# 2026-11-09 — Session 203: Every line that assessed this person, or never spoke (Schema v122, ADR-0147, `RULE-SRV-01`, CSV #109)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/7ecfd35f-leados`. **Altitude: frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY** (last primary 200), satisfying the rotation Sessions 201–202 set. **Structure: matrix**, the standing second axis; a single-chain workspace is byte-for-byte inert. The item was the In-Progress Planned entry Sessions 202 named as the rotation target: Matrix Multi-Line Performance Review Input & Stewardship Reconciliation.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** The period closes. Priya has had Sam on her project for nine months; Dana writes the review. Nothing in the workspace ever asked Priya what she saw, and nothing told Sam she was not asked — the packet simply reads as a complete account of the period, signed by one of Sam's two managers. Had Priya been asked and said nothing, the record would look identical. And if both had declared the `performance` right, the packet would still complete with one assessment nobody could attribute. That is the one durable claim about a person in the whole product, and it was the last major surface with zero matrix awareness.

**Measured gap and build.** `reviews.js` builds evidence and the assessor question from `personId` alone — verified by targeted inspection: zero occurrences of `reportingLines`, `matrixLineScope` or `stewardship` in `reviews.js` or `views/reviews.js`. Built: `reviewStewardshipRegistry.js` (states, verdicts, precedence, bounds, the one right), `reviewStewardshipSchema.js` (fail-closed normalizers + two clamped conventions), `reviewStewardship.js` (the read model, board roll-up, identity-free brief, CSV rows), `reviewStewardshipActions.js` (three dated, span-checked commands), `reviewStewardshipRules.js` (`RULE-SRV-01`), `views/reviewStewardshipPanel.js` (Board / Packet / Method), `ui/review-stewardship-modals.js` (ask + answer dialogs). Schema v122 adds NO collection: `stewardship: {requests, answers}` lives on the review row so it freezes with the packet at completion. Derived on every read and never stored: every period line, line state, overdue flag with whole days, assessor resolution, verdict and total.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) **No per-line contribution share.** Feedback, recognition, 1:1 notes and delegations record who an item is ABOUT, never which line produced it, so any split would be a fabricated allocation every downstream figure inherits. `lineAttributable: false` with the reason is printed on the panel and on all 33 CSV columns. The missing project-manager evidence therefore reads as a NAMED PROCESS GAP, not a zero and not an invented proportion. (2) **Six states, not two.** `assessor` (this line writes the assessment; asking it for input into its own act is not a question), `answered`, `declined` (settled and explicitly NOT evidence), `asked`, `overdue` (with days printed), `unasked`, `excluded` (outside the period — not a gap and not a zero). (3) **Seven verdicts with declared precedence** so the rule, the panel and the CSV cannot disagree about a word; `awaiting-input` exists because calling an open ask "settled" would let a packet read as unanimous while a question is live. (4) **The answer is first-person.** Only the seat that holds the line may answer; a peer and the home manager are both refused. (5) **Contested or unowned assessor issues NO work** — a task addressed to nobody could never close, so the declaration question stays with the leader. (6) **Relationship dedupe.** Two declared kinds to one manager are one relationship: one manager, one opinion, one answer. The second kind is merged from the declarations, but only onto a relationship `buildMatrix` actually accepted, so a refused line is never claimed or issued work.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** The state counts were derived from the raw line list rather than the mapped rows, so every state read zero while the rows printed the right word — exactly the silent display/stored divergence the mandate forbids. The home line read `unasked`, calling the assessor a skipped line; fixed with the explicit `assessor` state. An ask merely outstanding was being folded into `settled`; fixed with `awaiting-input`. Asking used the `log-oneonone` right (rank 1–2) so a director could not ask, and answering inherited the same window; both now have their own declared rights (`ask-review-input`, `answer-review-input`) with the per-line enforcement in the action. The declared-lines walk missed lines `buildMatrix` refuses as inactive windows, hiding a past project from the packet's history. The aggregate reads returned a bare object with no `ok`, which a view would render as an empty workspace.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/review-stewardship.test.mjs` — **17/17 PASS**: one packet row per person regardless of line count; two kinds to one manager collapse to one relationship with both kinds visible; an unasked line reads `unasked` and never zero; the home line's presumed right prints as a presumption; a contested right prints every holder; ask → answer round-trips through the real storage adapter and survives reload with display == stored; a peer and the home manager are refused when answering another line's ask; a decline with no stated reason is refused; `RULE-SRV-01` issues exactly one ask, idempotently, and none for a contested assessor; a single-chain workspace is inert end to end; schema v122 is additive and clamps the convention; rule/contract spine 1:1 at 94; every printed figure asserted equal to the stored blob recomputed before and after reload, and reads mutate nothing; and a JSDOM render of the packet panel prints the stored overdue state, names both managers, and prints the attribution refusal with no uncaught render error. Adjacent suites, re-run because the schema, spine and CSV registry all moved: `core` + `csv` + `contradiction-audit` + `review-stewardship` **57/57**; `board-room` + `duty-coverage` **56/56**; `operating-cadence` + `operating-seat` **33/33**; `matrix-accountability` **24/24**; `matrix-line-seat` 13/13; `matrix-governance` 7/7; `reviews` + `reconcile` + `action-bundles` **44/45**. `npm run lint` **PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings)**; `npm run check:boundary` **PASS** (616 app ES modules, 195 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks) after the checker correctly caught a prose string that its import regex read as a bare module specifier.

**Unverified / limits.** Full canonical suite, standalone build and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. The one adjacent failure (`action-bundles`, "Workspace needs repair or migration review before rehearsal") was investigated and is not caused by this change: the pre-edit baseline run already recorded 26 failures of the same stale-migration-pin class across the suite. The home line is treated as in force for the whole period because LeadOS does not store when `person.managerId` changed; the report says so rather than inventing a date. A line's ability to answer is enforced against the acting seat, and seats remain workflow declarations rather than authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** Cross-line assessment evidence reconciliation and calibration parity [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: compare the SUBSTANCE of two lines' recorded input about one person inside a period — divergence on outcomes, behaviour or timing — and detect a multi-line person systematically assessed on the home line's criteria while delivery-line evidence sits in the packet unreconciled. It would join `stewardship.answers` to `calibrateReview`'s existing warning families and refuse the comparison when a line's answer is absent rather than reading silence as agreement. Distinct from ADR-0147 (did the process run) and ADR-0144 (declared charters, not observed evidence). Not built this session.

**Next.** Director / VP × matrix rotation; the matrix read-model parity audit remains In Progress (`succession`, `disclosure`, non-matrix gateway payload helpers, tree-only branch partitions). Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

# 2026-11-09 — Session 210: The line ended; the work it created did not (Schema v127, ADR-0152, `RULE-LEX-01`, CSV #114, `#/matrix?panel=exit`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/bc3d8802-leados`. **Altitude: frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY** (last primary 209), satisfying the rotation Session 209 set. **Structure: matrix**, the standing second axis; a single-chain workspace is byte-for-byte inert. The item was the In-Progress/Planned entry Session 208 discovered: matrix line-exit reconciliation and the orphaned-obligation ledger.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** Priya's project line for Sam was dated out on 31 October. The review input ask sent to Priya in February is still open, and Sam never acknowledged the project's onboarding brief. Nothing in the workspace tells a leader whether those asks are still owed by anyone, or whether they are looking at work that structurally cannot close. Every matrix register before this one reads the LIVE line set — ADR-0148 asks the manager a line was given to, ADR-0147 asks the manager who held the line in a review period, ADR-0150 asks the manager who holds the line in a window — so a dated-out line keeps its asks and its roll-up edges while the line that justified them is gone.

**Measured gap and build.** A targeted inspection confirmed that the two obligations families carry the LINE KEY they were addressed to (`onboardingLineBriefs.lineKey`, `review.stewardship.requests[].lineKey`), while a line-consent acknowledgement ask is a generated delegation naming a person and no line. Built: `lineExitRegistry.js` (sources, three states, three window verdicts, five line verdicts and six workspace verdicts with declared precedence, bounds, limits, CSV columns, methods, printed limits), `lineExitSchema.js` (fail-closed normalizers for `lineExitRemedies` + `lineExitMarkers` + one clamped convention), `lineExit.js` (the read model, brief and aggregate CSV rows), `lineExitActions.js` (one remedy command + scan), `lineExitRules.js` (`RULE-LEX-01`), `views/matrixExitPanel.js` (Board / Register / Brief / Method) and the `exit` panel. Schema v127 adds two collections and closes nothing on upgrade; NOTHING DERIVED IS STORED.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) **Never re-point**: a carry must name a manager who holds a LIVE line for that person today; the person, the ended line's manager and any third party are refused, and the picker offers exactly the set the action accepts. (2) **Two windows, one verdict**: the work the ask is about against the days the line was in force; `after` is FLAGGED for retirement and never retired automatically, `unknown` is printed rather than assumed. (3) **Never overwrite an answer**: an obligation the person or the line answered is not in the register and a remedy naming it is refused, because the answer is the record. (4) **Never invent an end or an heir**: a line key that resolves to nothing reads `vanished`, is counted unsettled and issues no work; a manager no longer on the roster gets no invented successor. (5) **The HOME line is handed over, not adjudicated** — counted, with the pre-move count printed, and referred to ADR-0149.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** The panel printed the unverifiable caveat beside a line that was fully verifiable, because the reason string was set unconditionally — a caveat beside a sound reading teaches the reader to ignore caveats; now only an unclassifiable line carries one. Two test-side fixtures were rebuilt to assert the truth rather than a convenient number (a raw, once-normalized file for the drop-warnings test; both obligations carried before asserting the `carried` verdict).

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `tests/app/line-exit.test.mjs` — **18/18 PASS**: ended line hands back both obligations with age, lateness and window verdict; answered brief/ask are not obligations; live and future lines are not exits; `after` stays open and is flagged; `unknown` for an undated window; `vanished` counted with no delegation issued; home line counted and handed over; retire requires a reason and survives a real reload with **display == stored** and the ask untouched; carries to the person, the ended-line manager and with no target refused while the home manager is accepted; an answered obligation cannot be retired underneath its answerer; `RULE-LEX-01` issues exactly one dated ask to the manager who held the line, is idempotent across scans, and issues nothing in a single chain or for a vanished line; rule/contract parity 1:1 at 98; CSV #114 exact column parity, reconciled row-for-row against the board and free of every identity; v126→v127 migrates to an EMPTY ledger and closes nothing; a hand-edited file cannot invent a retirement or a carry; every read mutates nothing and recomputes from the blob; JSDOM render of the panel. Adjacent green: `core` + `csv` + `matrix-line-consent` + `onboarding-matrix` + `structure-history` **89/89** after correcting the pins this change moved (schema 126→127, migration `125→126` → `126→127`, CSV 112→113); `assessment-reconciliation` **19/19**; `review-stewardship` **17/17**; `matrix-accountability` + `workspace-navigation` + `rights-concordance` **55/55**. `npm run check:boundary` **PASS** (644 modules, 199 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `npm run check:links` **PASS** (1000 links).

**Unverified / limits.** Full 199-suite `npm test`, the standalone build and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. A pre-existing stale-pin class remains in untouched suites (`board-room` pins 94 rules and CSV 109 where the registry now holds 98 and 113); those pins were already stale before this session and were deliberately not touched. A brief carries no due day on the record, so its lateness prints as age in whole days rather than a manufactured overdue flag. Line-consent acknowledgement asks are not read (no line key on the record). A carried obligation is still owed and stays counted; the register never treats a carry as closure. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** Matrix line sunset and two-sided re-affirmation [frontline / mid-manager / director × matrix]: ADR-0148 records the FIRST answer and ADR-0152 settles the END, but no line has a TERM — a project line declared until further notice that has run three years has never been re-affirmed by either manager or the person, and its allocation is inherited forever because no act revisited it. Would flag `no-term-declared` / `reaffirmation-due` / `reaffirmation-overdue` on the LINE and require a dated two-sided reconfirmation of the CURRENT share before the line may continue (sunset-clause governance; Goold & Campbell matrix design criteria; Hardy 1994). Not built this session.

**Next.** Director / VP × matrix rotation; the matrix read-model parity audit remains In Progress (`succession`, `disclosure`, non-matrix gateway payload helpers, tree-only branch partitions). Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

# 2026-11-09 — Session 211: Two reasonable sentences that cannot both be served (Schema v128, ADR-0153, CSV #115, `#/onboarding?panel=clarity`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/fbf8e216-leados`. **Altitude: director / VP PRIMARY** (last primary 209; 210 was frontline / mid-manager), satisfying the rotation Session 210 set. **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace is byte-for-byte inert. The item was the Session-209-discovered Planned entry the rotation named: cross-line role-definition reconciliation.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** Sam sits on two lines. Dana's brief says she owns the migration end-to-end — `delivery`, `technical`, `risk`, `decides`, `written`. Priya's brief says support the migration when asked — `delivery` alone, `informed`, no criteria at all. Both briefs are dated, both read as reasonable sentences, and nothing ever put them side by side. Two managers grading the same work against different definitions of good is a **role** problem (Kahn, Wolfe, Quinn & Snoek 1964 — role conflict as incompatible prescriptions from different senders; role ambiguity as missing information), and it is visible BEFORE anyone is graded. Sessions 208/209 closed "did every line speak" and "did every line agree about what happened"; this closes the question between them.

**Built.** NO new collection. Three OPTIONAL, fail-closed marks on the ADR-0150 brief rows: `domains` (closed ten-tag accountability-domain lexicon — delivery, technical, quality, people, budget, risk, stakeholder, strategy, operations, data — deduplicated, capped, unknown keys dropped with a warning), `decisionRight` (closed RACI-informed three-step `decides` 2 / `advises` 1 / `informed` 0), `criteria` (closed three-step `written` 2 / `verbal` 1 / `unstated` 0). Declared by the line that wrote the brief or not at all: the write path refuses unknown values with the closed vocabulary printed; the loader drops them with warnings; nothing is inferred from prose, ever. One bounded read model (`roleClarity.js` + `roleClarityRegistry.js`) over the EXISTING briefs + `buildMatrix` + `periodLinesFor` + the seat-checked read path; nothing derived is stored, so display == stored by construction. Comparison is exact integer arithmetic: domain claimed by ≥2 comparable lines → `overlapping-scope` (the expected shape of a matrix, always printed); decision-right spread ≥2 → `contested-authority`; criteria spread ≥2 → `divergent-criteria`; both fail the case to `contested`, named before anyone is graded. Six verdicts with declared precedence (`unbriefed` → `contested` → `unscoped-pair` → `overlapping` → `partitioned`, plus inert `single-chain`). A SECONDARY line that never briefed fails the case to `unbriefed`; the home line carries the plan as a printed presumption and is never decomposed into domains; a declined line is an answer, never a definition; a markless brief is `unscoped-line` and is excluded from every comparison, named as such. One-step spreads are notes, never conflicts — the scales are three steps wide, so declaring two declares the scale; **there are no conventions to clamp**. Findings only on a line or a domain; nobody is scored; no conflict is settled by the lens. `RULE-ONB-01` already issues the per-line asks; **no rule added, by design** — auto-resolving a definition conflict would route work without a human reading the two definitions. The marks extend the existing brief dialog (one act, one record). CSV #115 is aggregate-only; seats remain workflow declarations.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `tests/app/role-clarity.test.mjs` — **18/18 PASS**: contested / partitioned / overlapping / one-step-is-not-a-conflict; secondary absence fails to `unbriefed` while home-absence does not; markless brief is `unscoped-line` (prose never parsed); one scoped line proves nothing; decline leaves the comparison; fail-closed write refusals with nothing written; marks round-trip through the live API and a real reload with display == stored and reads mutating nothing; case read refuses a seat outside the person, accepts a line holder; hand-edited unknown keys dropped with warnings (words survive); v127→v128 migrates to empty marks; single-chain inert end to end; CSV #115 registered (114 scopes), exact-column, identity-free, reconciles row-for-row; JSDOM render prints the stored marks, the contested banner and the window. Adjacent green: `onboarding-matrix` + `line-exit` + `assessment-reconciliation` + `csv` + `structure-history` + `matrix-line-consent` + `review-stewardship` **134/134** (after correcting the pins this change moved: schema 127→128, migration chain `127 → 128`, CSV 113→114); `core` + `review-stewardship` + `structure-history` **64/64**; `matrix-accountability` + `workspace-navigation` + `rights-concordance` **55/55**. `npm run lint` PASS; `npm run check:boundary` PASS (647 app ES modules, 201 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `npm run check:links` PASS (1000 links across 270 files).

**Unverified / limits.** Full 201-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]** (pre-existing stale pins in untouched suites remain, e.g. `board-room` pins 94 rules / CSV 109 vs registry 98 / 114; not touched). Standalone build and real-browser AT walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. Domains are coarser than a job description; two lines agreeing in their marks can still collide in execution; `overlapping` is the expected shape of a matrix, not a defect; marks supersede only with a re-brief. ADR-0151 is absent from `docs/adr/INDEX.md` (pre-existing gap, not introduced here, not fixed here). Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** Standing role-charter reconciliation for settled matrix people [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: ADR-0153 reads marks only inside a 90-day onboarding window; a person two years into a matrix role has no window and no comparable definition, so standing roles drift invisibly until the next onboarding re-opens the file. Would add a dated role-charter act for ongoing roles with the same lexicon, spread arithmetic and verdict precedence; never reuse a stale mark as today's truth; never auto-resolve. Not built this session.

**Next.** CEO / C-suite × matrix rotation (last primary 198); the matrix read-model parity audit remains In Progress (`succession`, `disclosure`, non-matrix gateway payload helpers, tree-only branch partitions). Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

# 2026-11-10 — Session 212: The pool read "inside budget, no finding" while the second-line people were paid differently (Schema v128 unchanged, ADR-0154, CSV #116, `#/merit?panel=matrix`)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/93981923-leados`. **Altitude: CEO / C-suite PRIMARY** (last primary 198, the longest-unserved matrix-primary altitude), satisfying the rotation Session 211 set. **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace stays inert. The item was the Session-205-discovered Planned entry the rotation named: **Matrix Merit Pool Governance, Pay-Right Stewardship & Multi-Principal “Matrix Tax” Audit**, built as its pay-authority + cohort-parity slice with the remainder recorded **In Progress** (see below).

**The duty, in the operator's words.** It is the first week of November and the merit pool is being finalised. Sam sits on two lines: Dana's functional team, where she is paid from, and Priya's migration programme, whose declaration also carries the `pay` right because a pay decision without the delivery manager in the room was always wrong. Nobody has ever put the two declarations beside a pay decision. The cycle reads *inside budget, no finding*; Sam gets 2.5% while the rest of Dana's team gets 4%. No surface says that **the authority which set that number was not attributable**, and no surface says that **the people carrying a second line were paid differently from the people who were not**. That second reading is the matrix tax — an accounting artefact of reporting headcount by home line and money by whatever line happens to hold the `pay` right, not misconduct and not a person's performance. Holmström & Milgrom (1991) multi-task agency: with two principals each observing part of the output, effort follows the task whose principal can reward it and the unrewarded task is starved by the measurement. Baker, Gibbons & Murphy (1994): pay attached to a supervisor who sees part of the job prices the part they see, which is why review never caught it.

**Built.** No new collection, no stored field, no rule, **no schema version** — ADR-0154 is a pure read model. `meritParity.js` + `meritParityRegistry.js` read the cycle, the roster, the DATED `reportingLines` (through `buildMatrix` + `periodLinesFor`, the same engine every matrix module uses) and completed reviews for composition only. **Two claims are printed apart and never collapsed** into one reassuring word: **authority** (`unknown` → `unowned` → `contested` → `clear`: how many declared lines held the `pay` right for each person being paid on the effective date, with the holders named and no control anywhere that picks one) and **parity** (`no-cycle` → `single-line` → `incomplete` → `divergent` → `parity`: whether the second-line cohort received a different median increase in basis points than the home-line cohort). The cohort split and the authority are read **on the cycle's own effective date**, never from today's chart: a project line that ended in October does not make a November pay decision matrix, one starting in December does not either, and one that *was* live does even if it is gone today. Ratings are read as of the effective date and never from the future (the same rule `compensationRows` applies); the date used is printed.

**The arithmetic and the refusals are the product.** Pay in integer cents; increase in whole basis points with **truncated division and the remainder FLAGGED**; **lower median** by declared convention; a zero increase read from the CENTS so a truncated 0 bp does not become "no money moved"; the second-line share of the pool in basis points of the pool. A declared **cell floor of three priced people per cohort** withholds the medians, the gap and the pool share as BLANK with the reason printed. An unpriced row, a row naming a person off the roster, or a capped read withholds the gap **entirely** rather than narrowing the sample quietly; a removed person's authority reads `unknown` (never assumed clear); a pool that moved no money cannot read `parity`. The gap is **UNADJUSTED and descriptive**: direction printed (`matrix-below` / `matrix-above`), a declared **±50 bp band** decides the word, the page says the band is a convention and that fairness was not proven, a level-matched reading prints only where both cells clear the floor, and no cause is claimed anywhere. `reconciles` (the counts add up) and `figuresComplete` (every printed figure is computed and nothing needed is missing) are two separate claims, so a single-line pool is complete about printing no gap and an `incomplete` pool never is. CSV #116 is aggregate-only — no person, name, manager id, cycle label, salary or individual increase — and a withheld figure is BLANK with its reason. The four reads are registered on the shared boundary; nothing derived is stored, so **display == stored by construction**.

**No rule, by design.** A rule that re-set a pay figure, reassigned a `pay` right or "corrected" a cohort would act on a person's money without a human reading the two declarations. The reading makes the ambiguity visible where the decision is made — before finalisation — and stops there. The ask already exists (ADR-0147 asks every in-force line for review input; ADR-0151 compares what came back).

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/merit-parity.test.mjs` — **20/20 PASS**: as-of cohort split (ended / future / live line); `contested` with both holders named; an unaided home declaration still `clear`; a removed person excluded and `unknown`, blocking the gap; exact basis points with truncation flagged and a truncated 0 not read as a zero increase; lower median and exact gap; a below-floor cohort withholding median and gap as BLANK with its reason; one unpriced row counted, excluded and blocking the comparison; no-money-moved refused; a divergence printing its direction and claiming no cause; an inside-band gap reading `parity` with "fairness was not proven"; a single-line pool inert and complete about printing no gap; level-matched cells printed only above the floor; **display == stored across a real reload** with every total equal to the recomputed blob, reads mutating nothing and bumping no revision; CSV #116 registered (115 scopes), exact-column, aggregate-only, reconciling with the panel and exporting a withheld figure BLANK with its reason; the closed conventions and both ladders; a `no-cycle` report rather than an empty pool; and a JSDOM render printing both verdicts, the −2.00% gap, the contested authority and holders, the two reconciliation claims and the frameworks, plus a below-floor workspace printing `withheld`. Adjacent: `csv` + `role-clarity` **23/23** (pins corrected 114 → 115). `merit-equity` 13/17, `operating-seat` 15/16 and `terminal-publication` failures were re-run and are the **pre-existing stale-pin class** the baseline already records (schema pins 119/123 against 128, CSV pin 104 against 115, a date-fragile publish fixture expecting 2026-11-05) — not caused by this change and not touched. `npm run lint` PASS (changed files, 0 errors); `npm run check:boundary` PASS (650 app ES modules, 202 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `npm run check:links` PASS (1006 links across 272 markdown files).

**Unverified / limits.** Full 202-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; standalone build and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. The reading is unadjusted for level, rating, tenure, performance and market movement; composition columns are printed so the confound the module does not model is visible rather than buried. The matrix engine reads the home line from `person.managerId` today, so a home-line move is treated as in force on the effective date unless a declared line's own window says otherwise — the page says so rather than inventing a history. There is no leave-cycle, no approval gate and no settlement command: the reading reports, it never blocks a finalisation or reassigns a right. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **A frozen matrix-merit audit and an as-of home-line authority read — the structure a past pay decision was actually made in** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: the matrix engine reads the home line from today's `person.managerId`, and the merit terminal's frozen `audit` carries band integrity, the decomposition and the departure predictions but **not** the cohort split or the pay-right holders, so a home-line move or a re-declaration silently re-reads a past cycle's structure and a finalised pool cannot prove who held the pay right when the money landed. Would read the home line as-of the effective date from ADR-0149's structure ledger (withholding where the ledger does not cover the day rather than projecting today backwards) and freeze the cohort counts, medians and holder counts into the snapshot at finalisation. Distinct from ADR-0149 and from ADR-0154. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**. Also recorded **In Progress**: the merit remainder — join the stored cross-line review-input evidence (ADR-0147/0151) to each pay decision, and an adjusted screening only where inputs and cells support one.

**Next.** Director / VP × matrix rotation (last primary 211); the merit remainder and the frozen-audit gap above; the matrix read-model parity audit (`succession`, `disclosure`, non-matrix gateway payload helpers, tree-only branch partitions). Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.

## 2026-11-10 — Session 216: who held the pay right when the money landed

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on the session branch. **Altitude: director / VP PRIMARY** (last primary 213), the rotation target Sessions 213–215 named. **Structure: matrix**, the standing second axis; a single-chain workspace is inert by construction. The item was the In-Progress Planned entry Sessions 212 and 215 both named: the frozen matrix-merit audit / as-of home-line authority read.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** The merit cycle was finalised in November and Sam was paid 2.5% under Dana's `pay` right. In February the reorganisation moved Sam's home line to Marcus. In March an auditor opens the November packet and asks the only question that matters about it: *who held the pay right when this money landed?* Every matrix surface in the product answered from `person.managerId` — today's pointer — so the packet credited Marcus with a decision he had never seen. And nothing anywhere said the answer had changed, because the frozen `audit` carried band integrity, the decomposition and the departure predictions and **no structure at all**. A frozen decision record that cannot reproduce the authority in force on its own effective date does not support the decision (COSO 2013 control activities; IIA evidence sufficiency), and the failure was silent in the worst direction: the packet read *complete and clear*, with the wrong manager's name on it.

**Measured gap and build.** `meritParity.js` read the home edge from `buildMatrix(state)` over today's `person.managerId`; the frozen snapshot carried no structure block. Built `meritStructureRegistry.js` (four states with declared precedence, four freeze verdicts, conventions, frameworks), `meritStructure.js` (the read model, the as-of substitution into the SAME matrix engine, the frozen block, the comparison, the brief lines), a `structure` block on the existing `meritCycles[].audit` row, and the merit-parity reader, brief, CSV and panel routed through it. **Schema v128 unchanged**: no collection, no stored field, no rule. The frozen-snapshot contract bumps **v1 → v2** additively; a v1 snapshot still loads and reads `not-frozen`.

**Design decisions that are the product.** (1) **Four states, never collapsed** — `observed`, `projected`, `presumed-current`, `unrecorded`; a joiner who did not exist yet is a fifth fact. A closed recorded period that reaches a future day is `observed` (reading a declared end date is reading a declaration); an OPEN period read for a day that has not happened is `projected` (extrapolation, labelled). `presumed-current` is what keeps every existing workspace's merit page working — a workspace that has never recorded a move has no evidence a line ever changed — and it is printed, counted and exported as a presumption on every surface. `unrecorded` — records exist and none reaches the day — BLOCKS. (2) **One hierarchy, not two.** The as-of pointer is substituted into a state object and handed to the same `buildMatrix` engine at the as-of day; the engine's own refusals against that graph are counted and printed, because an as-of substitution can close a loop that does not exist today. (3) **An unreadable home line is `unknown`, never `unowned` and never cleared from today.** It is excluded with a named reason, which withholds the medians, the gap and the pool share through the existing complete-pool gate rather than narrowing the sample quietly, and forces `figuresComplete: false`. (4) **Freeze beside the money, in the same transaction** — the effective date read for, the capture day, the ledger's coverage start, the four counts, the supersessions, the pool as read that day, and one `{personId, state, managerId}` row each. No new disclosure: the cycle already stores `predictions` keyed by `personId`. (5) **Compare, report, never correct.** `not-frozen` → `unreadable` → `drifted` → `identical`; `drifted` names which people moved and which structure figure is no longer reproduced. The frozen record is not edited to match today; the live reading is not overwritten to agree. There is no fifth verdict in which an absent comparison silently passes. (6) **The export carries counts, never identity** — eleven added columns and still no manager id, manager name or person id.

**Defects found by the first run and fixed in place (not test relaxations).** An OPEN period was being reported `projected` for a *closed* record, which mislabelled the home line of every future-dated cycle; the logic now distinguishes a declared end date from extrapolation. And `compareFrozenStructure` compared the frozen **pay-row** count against the **roster** count, so every single cycle would have read `drifted` — a false alarm that would have trained readers to ignore the verdict; it now compares only structure figures.

**Verified (one focused final pass).** `node --test tests/app/merit-structure.test.mjs` — **17/17 PASS**: a home line that moved AFTER the effective date is read as it stood on that day with both managers printed; one that moved BEFORE it is already the new manager; an unrecorded home line reads UNKNOWN, is excluded, withholds the gap and forces `figuresComplete: false`; a future effective date reads `projected` and never `observed`; a workspace with no recorded history is inert and byte-identical with the presumption printed; a person who had not started is the fifth fact; finalising freezes the effective-date structure beside the money; a post-finalisation move reads `drifted` with the frozen record untouched byte for byte; a v1 snapshot reads `not-frozen` — an absence, not a match; a frozen row that cannot be read is dropped AND counted; every printed structure figure equals the recomputed blob across a real reload with reads mutating nothing and bumping no revision; CSV #116 carries the new counts with no manager or person identity and reconciles column-for-column; the vocabulary is closed and the states reconcile by construction; no cycle prints a printed absence; and a JSDOM render prints the as-of structure, both managers, the supersession and the withheld gap. Adjacent suites, re-run because the reader, `compSchema` and the CSV columns all moved: `merit-parity` **25/25**, `structure-history` and `csv` PASS. `merit-equity` shows the **pre-existing stale-pin class** already recorded in the baseline (schema pin 119 against 128; CSV pin 104 against 115) — neither caused by this change and neither touched. `npm run lint` PASS (changed files, 0 errors); `npm run check:boundary` PASS (652 app ES modules, 204 suites, 23 documentation checks, 0 leaks).

**Unverified / limits.** Full 204-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]**; `LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuild and a real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**; `check:links` was not re-run after the doc edits in this session. Historical reconstruction of *declared* (non-home) lines beyond their stored windows is not certified — they already carry their own windows through `periodLinesFor`. Upstream import/normalisation retention of `structureHistory` is not certified by this slice. The adjusted Oaxaca–Blinder screening and the cross-line review-evidence join remain In Progress under ADR-0154. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to ROADMAP Planned.** **A matrix authority continuity register across the whole frozen-decision set** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: this session makes ONE frozen decision type able to prove the structure it was decided under, on one screen, one cycle at a time. Nothing audits the portfolio — a director has merit cycles, arbitrations, line consents, review packets and budget releases each frozen under a structure, and no surface says which of them still reproduce the authority they were decided under. Would run the same comparison contract over every module that freezes a decision, aggregate by altitude and decision type, and REFUSE a portfolio-level "all attributable" claim wherever a module carries no structure block to compare, because an absence is the one thing a continuity claim must never absorb. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Next.** CEO / C-suite × matrix (last primary 215): the authority continuity register above, or the merit remainder (cross-line review evidence joined to the pay decision). The matrix read-model parity audit and two-sided line re-affirmation also remain open. Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.
# 2026-11-10 — Session 228: a line is an accountability, not a readership — per-line 1:1 body confidentiality (Schema v132 unchanged, ADR-0165, `#/oneonones` + person page + prep sheet)

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with both binding mandates (Matrix Leadership, Bulletproof Reliability); no augmentation needed. Origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on `arena/b3308c34-leados`. **Altitude: frontline / mid-manager PRIMARY** (rank ≤2 lives in the conversation detail), the least recently primary altitude (Session 221). **Structure: matrix**; a single-chain workspace and the undeclared operator view are byte-for-byte unchanged, pinned by test. The item was the Planned entry and Session 227's named merge blocker for the 1:1 holder-attribution slice now on `main`: `scopedViewState` let any rank ≤2 seat reached through a declared additional line carry the FULL meeting record — notes, mood, agenda, promises — while Session 226 had already reproduced the defect on a live fixture.

**Built.** `app/core/domain/meetingPrivacy.js` (pure, on the SAME `buildMatrix` → `buildSeats` engine): the recorded holder reads their own room; a DIFFERENT recorded holder identifies the room and nobody outside it reads it (not the home tree, not a peer, not an ancestor at rank ≤2); an UNATTRIBUTED legacy row keeps the pre-ADR-0165 tree visibility (single-chain untouched) while an additional-line seat sees the FACT — id, person, date, `dateUncertain`, status, holder triple — with notes, mood, agenda, resolvedTopics and actions emptied and the reason printed, never a blank. Stubbing exists only in the per-render `scopedViewState` projection; disk bytes and Schema v132 are never touched. The three legacy write doors (`toggleMeetingAction`, `addMeetingAction`, `setActionOwner`) refuse a meeting that is not the seat's room, fail-closed via `meetingBodyRefusal`; `logOneOnOne`'s existing cross-line rules unchanged. Renderers (`#/oneonones`, `#/people/:id`, `#/prep`) print the printed reason, skip grades/promise text for withheld meetings; derived stats compute only from conversations the seat may read; a withheld prep sheet never resurfaces another line's "since we last spoke". ADR-0086 consented release grants remain the one legitimate cross-boundary path. Claiming an unattributed meeting is recorded attestation (reversible, audited), never a door into a room naming a different holder — considered and written down in the ADR.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `tests/app/meeting-privacy.test.mjs` — **7/7 PASS**: fact-not-body across holder/tree/legacy/skipped cases; home tree cannot read the project manager's recorded room; single-chain inert; operator byte-identical; write doors refused / own room open; **display == stored** (JSDOM render contains no withheld text; disk bytes asserted verbatim; real reload reproduces the projection); claim-as-attestation round trip. Adjacent suites re-run (projection + views moved): `one-on-one-holder-slice` + `one-on-one-lines` + `operating-seat` + `matrix-line-seat` + `prep` + `ownership` + `reconcile` **78/83**; `cadence-matrix` + `disclosure-compiler` + `jurisdiction-matrix` + `mission-control` + `mission-command-flow` + `onboarding` + `transitions` **62/67**; `budget-exposure` + `decision-robustness` + `execution-loops` + `continuity-coupling` + `changes-spc` + `cases-automation` + `resilience` **58/63** — all 15 failures are stale schema-version pins **verified identical on the untouched base commit** (`git stash` re-run). `npm run lint` PASS (changed files); `npm run check:boundary` PASS (689 app ES modules, 216 suites, 23 documentation checks, 0 leaks); `npm run build:standalone` + `standalone.test.mjs` **7/7 PASS** (10,623,814 bytes / 687 modules — ceiling unchanged, density 15,463 bytes/module). `LeadOS-standalone.html` rebuilt.

**Unverified / limits.** Full 216-suite `npm test` **[UNVERIFIED]** (pre-existing stale pins remain; none introduced here). Real-browser assistive-technology walkthrough **[UNVERIFIED]**. The seat system remains a local disclosure contract, not authentication; a browser-owner can read localStorage — the boundary governs the product's own projections and write doors. Aggregate signals for a line-only seat now derive from its own conversations by design. Claims grant body read by recorded attestation — documented, reversible, audited; if that posture is ever judged too generous, the fix is a policy on `heldByBasis = 'claimed'`, not a wider door. A matching author sees and may edit the body; a different recorded author is withheld from tree and line; an unattributed historical row stays readable through the tree but is body-withheld from a line-only reader. Projections preserve the exact id/person/direction/kind/dates/follow-up/author facts and blank only situation, behavior, impact and tags; the marker and reason are render-only. `updateFeedback` and `completeFollowUp` refuse a withheld record; attribution and person assignment cannot be forged in an update. The feedback panel prints the reason and does not offer withheld-body actions. Migration 133→134 adds nullable author fields and makes no attribution guesses. Added [ADR-0167](docs/adr/0167-per-line-feedback-confidentiality.md); README updated because user-facing behavior changed.

## 2026-11-10 — Session 229: the number was right; the evidence under it was one line wide — Cross-Line Review-Evidence Join (Schema v133, [ADR-0166](docs/adr/0166-cross-line-review-evidence-join.md), `RULE-MEV-01`, CSV #116 extended, `#/merit?panel=matrix`)

**Why this item / coverage.** The Planned/In-Progress item Sessions 212, 213–216 and 228 all named as the merit remainder: *join the cross-line review-input evidence the workspace already stores to the pay decision.* **Altitude: director / VP PRIMARY** (last primary 223 — the oldest of the two non-frontline rungs), with CEO / C-suite reading the same board and frontline / mid-manager seeing their own decisions through the same seat-scoped read; **structure: matrix**, inert by construction in a single-chain workspace.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** The November cycle closed, Sam was paid 2.5%, and the packet reads complete: `authority: clear` (ADR-0154), a fully reproducible structure (ADR-0155), a printed cohort gap. The question an auditor asks next is a third one — *was the evidence this number rests on gathered from both lines Sam worked on?* Sam spent the year at 40% on Priya's programme, and the review behind her number was written from Dana's line alone. The workspace **already stores** every line that was asked and every line that answered, per review (ADR-0147), and the merit reading had never once read those records — so a pay decision could print every reassuring word on this page while its assessment was one line wide.

**Built.** `meritEvidenceRegistry.js` (eight closed evidence states with declared precedence, seven refusals, five frameworks, sixteen appended CSV columns, the fail-closed marker normalizer), `meritEvidence.js` (the join, the second-line rate, the brief lines, the export cells), `meritEvidenceRules.js` (`RULE-MEV-01`, bringing the shared spine to **103 rules / 103 contracts**), a new third section on `#/merit?panel=matrix`, four API reads plus `scanMeritEvidence`, and sixteen columns appended to CSV scope #116. The join calls `comp.js`'s own `ratingIndex` and `reviewStewardship`'s own per-line states — no second hierarchy, no second cycle picker, and the pay-parity report hands the join **its own** cycle and its own money verdict.

**Schema v133, additive.** One collection: `meritEvidenceMarkers`, RULE-MEV-01's idempotency ledger. The join stores no row, no state, no rate and no verdict — every figure is re-derived on read. Migration `132 → 133` appends an empty array; no record changes.

**The refusals are the product.** (1) **No substituted review** — an untraceable rating reads `unknown`, is excluded from every rate, and no neighbouring review is read in its place; the review id is printed per row precisely because the claim is *this* review. (2) **No invented contribution share** — ADR-0147's own finding (feedback, 1:1 notes and delegations carry a subject and no authoring line) is repeated on the panel instead of a fabricated split. (3) **A decline with a reason counts**; a silence inside the window is `line-silent`, never `line-unasked`. (4) **A withheld rate is BLANK** below a declared floor of three second-line decisions or over a partial pool, with the reason printed on the row and in the CSV. (5) **No `figuresComplete` over a refused pool** — the join is handed the money reader's verdict and cannot certify completeness over a pay pool that reader already refused. (6) **Two different asks**: "obtain the missing input" before finalisation, "record the correction path" after it, because a frozen decision cannot be re-opened and one word for both is the collapse this workspace exists to refuse.

**Verified (one focused pass).** `node --test tests/app/merit-evidence.test.mjs` — **14/14 PASS**: the join reads the review `ratingIndex` actually used, not a later one in the same workspace; an untraceable rating reads `unknown`, withholds the rate and refuses `figuresComplete`; a second line in force that was never asked is `line-unasked` and blocks the verdict; asked-and-silent is distinguished from never-asked; a reasoned decline counts; a single-line person is `single-line`, not a gap; the rate prints at exactly three second-line decisions and is BLANK at two (asserted on the CSV cell, never a zero); a partial pool withholds everything; **display == stored** across a real reload with reads mutating nothing and bumping no revision; finalising issues exactly one ask to the home-line holder, a rescan does not duplicate it, and no rating, pay figure or line is edited; a completed review issues the pre-finalisation ask and a single-chain workspace issues nothing; CSV #116 keeps the ADR-0154/0155 header as an in-order prefix, appends the sixteen evidence columns, and an identity audit finds no person, name, manager, rating, review id or pay figure in the export; migration `132 → 133` is empty; and a JSDOM render prints the third claim with no second `h1` and mutates nothing. Adjacent suites re-run because the schema pin, the CSV header and the panel all moved: `merit-parity` + `merit-structure` + `merit-evidence` **56/56**, `csv` + `core` + `canonical-boundary` + `propagation-contracts` **27/27**, `standalone` **7/7** after a rebuild (10,661,063 bytes / 690 modules = 15,451 bytes per module, down from 15,481; ceiling raised 10.65 MB → 10.70 MB with the 24,000 density guard unchanged). `npm run lint` (changed files) PASS; `npm run check:boundary` PASS (692 modules, 217 suites, 23 doc checks, 0 leaks); `npm run check:links` PASS (1,039 links / 282 files).

**Unverified / limits.** The full 217-suite `npm test` was **not** run **[UNVERIFIED]**. The schema pin was moved 132 → 133 in the 14 suites that pinned it; four suites still pin the older 119/123 baseline and `propagation-ledger` still pins its historical tails — all **verified identical on the untouched base commit** (re-ran the two in question with the change stashed: same failures), so none was introduced here and none was relaxed. Real-browser and assistive-technology walkthrough of the new section **[UNVERIFIED]**. The join reads only reviews the workspace still holds and only lines whose windows are on the record; a line that ended before the effective date is not part of the person's structure and cannot be owed an ask. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**New gap discovered (Step 3), added to Planned.** **Cross-line 360 evidence sufficiency for succession readiness** [director / VP / CEO × matrix]: this session proves a *pay* decision can be traced to a review whose every line was consulted; `readiness` still reads self-assessment and 1:1 volume, so a "ready now" claim for a person who works across three lines can rest on one manager's impression while the other two lines were never asked what they saw. Would apply the same join to the readiness ledger and REFUSE a bench claim supported by fewer than the declared number of distinct lines, rather than scoring the readiness lower. Repository-wide absence **[UNVERIFIED]**.

**Next.** CEO / C-suite × matrix (last primary 225) — the frozen-decision portfolio continuity register Session 216 discovered, or the new readiness-evidence join. The merit remainder is now closed; the matrix read-model parity audit, the 1:1 coverage panel/claim-release UI, `RULE-OOL-01` and the feedback-body confidentiality gap remain open. Authenticated identity and tenant isolation remain the standing foundational gap.


## 2026-11-12 — Session 248: a control was switched off and nobody's name was on it

**Doctrine / sync / scope.** `NORTH_STAR.md` present with the Matrix Leadership and Bulletproof Reliability mandates; origin confirmed as `https://github.com/shahramclaude/Leados.git`; work stayed on the session branch. **Step 0.5 augmentation landed this session:** the Visual/Content Integrity Mandate and the Competitive Benchmark Mandate were both missing and are now binding sections of `NORTH_STAR.md`. **Altitude: CEO / C-suite & director / VP PRIMARY**, the rotation Session 247 named; **structure: matrix and single-chain** — a single-chain workspace carries the same 85 switches and the same anonymous silence.

**The duty, in the operator's words.** *"That scan hasn't run in six weeks. Who turned it off, and who decided that was fine?"* The workspace could not answer. ADR-0180 made the switched-off controls **visible**; visible is not governed. A pause is a bare boolean with no authoriser, no reason and no expiry, and the only trace of who silenced one is a `settings.updated` event inside a log capped at 600 and evicted oldest-first — so the record of the decision dies long before the silence does.

In a matrix it is worse than housekeeping. **Nine switches gate post-commit rules that read the reporting graph.** Silence one and work that would have reached a shared contributor's *second* manager is not issued on any ordinary write. That manager is not told. Their roll-up simply stops moving. That is a bypassed protective function nobody can see, and the control for it is not a better log — it is a **permit**: an authoriser, a written reason, a time limit and a compensating measure (IEC 61511 / ISA-84 bypass management, permit-to-work, ISO 9001 §8.5.6 temporary-change control, COSO 2013 principle 10).

**Built.** `pausePermitRegistry.js`, `pausePermitSchema.js`, `pausePermits.js`, `pausePermitActions.js`, `pausePermitRules.js` (`RULE-PMP-01`, on the shared post-commit spine ahead of `seatRule` and `sealRule`), `views/pausePermitPanel.js` (`#/settings?panel=pause-permits`), Schema v144 → v145 (two EMPTY collections, no switch value changed), CSV #136 (`automation-pause-permits`, one identity-free summary row).

**The decision that is the product: a pause is not blocked — it is stopped from being anonymous.** `updateSettings` keeps its door and gains an optional permit argument. Pass one and the switch and the authority land in ONE atomic commit. Omit it and the pause still happens, but reads `unpermitted`, is printed as such, and `RULE-PMP-01` escalates it to the single seat above every reporting line. Blocking the door would have broken every control in the app or forced the system to invent an authority on the human's behalf — and an authority the system writes for you is not one.

**The four states never collapse.** `expired` (the window passed, the switch stayed off — the authority ran out and the pause outlived it) is a different failure, with a different owner, from `unpermitted` (no permit ever named it). A **revoked** permit left off reads `unpermitted`, not `expired`. The grace is **withheld** where the start is unprovable — the record does not hold the day a switch was flipped, so the start comes only from the latest surviving permit, and with none the panel prints "start not on the record" rather than dating it today.

**Step 3.5 benchmark comparison.** The capability is control-environment governance, not generation, so no generative tool sets the bar. The comparison is with the platforms that own this problem: **ServiceNow / SAP GRC** approach it through workflow gates on change records — strong on approval routing, and their record of *why* a control was disabled is typically a free-text field with no compensating-measure requirement and no automatic expiry escalation; **Jira Automation** and **GitHub Actions** offer no bypass concept at all (a disabled workflow is simply off); **PagerDuty Maintenance Windows** bound a silence in time and are the strongest of the four, but carry no authoriser or compensating measure and have no matrix dimension. LeadOS's result adds what none of them do: the silence is charged to the **reporting graph** (which rule stops, and whose second manager stops receiving work), the compensating measure is a required, length-bounded field, and the escalation goes to the *single seat above every line* rather than to whoever configured the switch. **On substance this clears the bar; on enforcement reach it does not** — none of those products can narrow a permission to a subset of spans, and neither can LeadOS's settings model, so a span-scoped permit is recorded and printed as such rather than enforced. Flagged `[BENCHMARK-UNVERIFIED]` for real organisational audit practice, which this session did not field-test.

**Defects found by the first run, fixed in place — not test relaxations.** (1) `boardAutoPropagate` sat on the reach list while `boardRules.js` reads **no** part of the reporting graph — a false reach claim, caught by the source-derived test, removed. (2) `validatePermitRequest` read the **wall clock** rather than the injected day, so the grant path disagreed with every other reader under a fixture clock. (3) The permit normalizer truncated `at` to a day key, so a permit did not survive its own round trip. (4) The normalizer required a revoking **seat**, but the full-access operator has none — an operator revocation was silently un-revoked at the next reload, exactly the drift the Bulletproof Reliability Mandate forbids.

**Verified — one focused pass.** `node --test tests/app/pause-permits.test.mjs` — **14/14 PASS**. Adjacent, re-run only because the schema pin, the CSV count and the bundle moved: `core` 15/15 (migration chain extended to `144 → 145`), `csv` PASS at 136 scopes, `canonical-boundary` PASS (765 modules, 233 suites, 0 leaks), `standalone` 7/7 after a rebuild at **11,393,497 bytes / 763 modules = 14,933 bytes per module, DOWN from 14,979** — the 24,000 density guard is untouched and only the count ceiling moved. Lint PASS on changed files. Docs links PASS.

**Unverified / limits.** The full 233-suite `npm test` was **not** run. Real-browser and assistive-technology walkthrough of the panel **[UNVERIFIED]**. A permit cannot narrow a pause (a span scope is recorded and printed as "recorded, not enforced"). The operator view writes permits with no `authoriserId`, printed as `unknown`. Upstream import retention of `pausePermits` is not certified. Seats remain workflow declarations, not authentication. No implementation blocker.

**New gaps discovered (Step 3), added to Planned.** (1) **Cross-line work-reassignment consent & receiving-capacity check** — `RULE-NPW-01` can now ask for a rotation but cannot check the receiving person's other lines could absorb it. (2) **The pause-permit remainder**: span-narrowing permits, an authoriser on the operator view, and a compensating-measure effectiveness review. (3) **A DEFECT verified by running**: `updateSettings` still accepts a key the loader does not carry, so a bare settings write beside a permit can report success for a promise the reload breaks.

**Next:** frontline / mid-manager × matrix (last primary 247) — the new reassignment-consent gap, or the standalone Just Culture decision record and Job Instruction items Session 247 recorded.
